On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against general knowledge.\n- Every finding carries a CWE id and a `file:line`.\n- The \"Skills applied\" list is present.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

The digest is treated as secret - it is never logged and never\n     8\t * returned to a caller of the service.\n     9\t */\n    10\tfinal class AccountCredentials {\n    11\t\n    12\t    private final String username;\n    13\t    private final byte[] salt;\n    14\t    private final int iterations;\n    15\t    private final byte[] digest;\n    16\t\n    17\t    AccountCredentials(String username, byte[] salt, int iterations, byte[] digest) {\n    18\t        this.username = username;\n    19\t        this.salt = salt;\n    20\t        this.iterations = iterations;\n    21\t        this.digest = digest;\n    22\t    }\n    23\t\n    24\t    String username() {\n    25\t        return username;\n    26\t    }\n    27\t\n    28\t    byte[] salt() {\n    29\t        return salt.clone();\n    30\t    }\n    31\t\n    32\t    int iterations() {\n    33\t        return iterations;\n    34\t    }\n    35\t\n    36\t    byte[] digest() {\n    37\t        return digest.clone();\n    38\t    }\n    39\t\n    40\t    /**\n    41\t     * Deliberately no serialiser. Rendering a credential line is provisioning, which is account\n    42\t     * creation, and this component is scoped to authentication and event logging: a shipped writer\n    43\t     * would be a production path that turns a record back into a file, and one more place where a\n    44\t     * digest could be written somewhere its owner did not approve. The test tree builds its own\n    45\t     * lines. {@link #toString()} likewise omits both secret fields, so an accidental log or exception\n    46\t     * message containing a record cannot publish a crackable verifier.\n    47\t     */\n    48\t    @Override\n    49\t    public String toString() {\n    50\t        return \"AccountCredentials[\" + username + \", iterations=\" + iterations + \"]\";\n    51\t    }\n    52\t}\nFILE AccountField.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     4\timport java.security.MessageDigest;\n     5\timport java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException;\n     6\t\n     7\t/**\n     8\t * Renders the account field of an audit record.\n     9\t *\n    10\t * <p>The username is never written to the log. What is written instead is\n    11\t * {@code account_id=<64 hex chars>} - the SHA-256 of the submitted name, ASCII-lower-cased - plus\n    12\t * {@code account_hint=<distinct-codepoint-count>} and {@code account_present=true|false}.\n    13\t *\n    14\t * <p>Why this is safe to keep:\n    15\t * <ul>\n    16\t *   <li>It is one-way. A log reader cannot recover the name, so a leaked log is not a list of\n    17\t *       accounts. The hash is <em>not</em> keyed: an offline dictionary of candidate names can be\n    18\t *       hashed and compared, which is why the README lists unsalted account identifiers as residual\n    19\t *       risk and why the field exists at all - the alternative is a log that cannot be searched.</li>\n    20\t *   <li>It is computed from the <em>submitted</em> text in every code path, so it never depends on\n    21\t *       the outcome of the check and cannot tell an attacker anything they can also learn from the\n    22\t *       response.</li>\n    23\t *   <li>Lower-casing makes {@code Alice} and {@code alice} the same identifier, matching the\n    24\t *       store's one-record-per-name rule.</li>\n    25\t *   <li>The hint is a number in {@code 0..32}, derived only from the submitted text; it exists so\n    26\t *       an operator can tell \"one account probed many times\" from \"many names probed once\" without\n    27\t *       storing the names.</li>\n    28\t * </ul>\n    29\t */\n    30\tfinal class AccountField {\n    31\t\n    32\t    private static final char[] HEX = \"0123456789abcdef\".toCharArray();\n    33\t\n    34\t    private AccountField() {\n    35\t    }\n    36\t\n    37\t    static String render(String submittedUsername, boolean present) {\n    38\t        return \"account_id=\" + accountId(submittedUsername)\n    39\t                + \" account_hint=\" + distinctCodePoints(submittedUsername)\n    40\t                + \" account_present=\" + (present ? \"true\" : \"false\");\n    41\t    }\n    42\t\n    43\t    static String accountId(String username) {\n    44\t        if (username == null) {\n    45\t            return repeat('0', 64);\n    46\t        }\n    47\t        byte[] canonical = lowerAscii(username).getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n    48\t        final byte[] digest;\n    49\t        try {\n    50\t            digest = MessageDigest.getInstance(\"SHA-256\").digest(canonical);\n    51\t        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException e) {\n    52\t            // SHA-256 is required of every conformant JRE; reaching this means the platform is\n    53\t            // broken and the attempt must not be logged with a guess.\n    54\t            throw new IllegalStateException(e);\n    55\t        }\n    56\t        char[] out = new char[digest.length * 2];\n    57\t        for (int i = 0; i < digest.length; i++) {\n    58\t            int b = digest[i] & 0xff;\n    59\t            out[i * 2] = HEX[b >>> 4];\n    60\t            out[i * 2 + 1] = HEX[b & 0x0f];\n    61\t        }\n    62\t        return new String(out);\n    63\t    }\n    64\t\n    65\t    /**\n    66\t     * Renders the size hint: how many <em>distinct</em> code points the submitted name used.\n    67\t     *\n    68\t     * <p>The set is a fixed 256-bit sketch rather than an exact set, so it saturates at 32 and two\n    69\t     * very different names can share a value. That is deliberate: the field exists to let a reviewer\n    70\t     * spot \"this name reuses one character 40 times\" without storing the name itself, and a\n    71\t     * collision loses nothing while an exact count would leak the alphabet of a name the log must\n    72\t     * not contain.\n    73\t     */\n    74\t    private static final int MAX_DISTINCT_CODE_POINT_HINT = 32;\n    75\t\n    76\t    static int distinctCodePoints(String username) {\n    77\t        if (username == null || username.isEmpty()) {\n    78\t            return 0;\n    79\t        }\n    80\t        int[] seen = new int[8];\n    81\t        int count = 0;\n    82\t        int length = username.length();\n    83\t        for (int i = 0; i < length;) {\n    84\t            int cp = username.codePointAt(i);\n    85\t            i += Character.charCount(cp);\n    86\t            int slot = (cp >>> 5) & 7;\n    87\t            int bit = cp & 31;\n    88\t            int mask = 1 << bit;\n    89\t            if ((seen[slot] & mask) != 0) {\n    90\t                continue;\n    91\t            }\n    92\t            seen[slot] |= mask;\n    93\t            count++;\n    94\t            if (count == MAX_DISTINCT_CODE_POINT_HINT) {\n    95\t                // Saturation, not truncation of the loop: a 40-character name and a 400-character\n    96\t                // name must not be distinguishable by this field.\n    97\t                return MAX_DISTINCT_CODE_POINT_HINT;\n    98\t            }\n    99\t        }\n   100\t        return count;\n   101\t    }\n   102\t\n   103\t    private static String lowerAscii(String value) {\n   104\t        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(value.length());\n   105\t        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n   106\t            char c = value.charAt(i);\n   107\t            if (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') {\n   108\t                c = (char) (c + 32);\n   109\t            }\n   110\t            out.append(c);\n   111\t        }\n   112\t        return out.toString();\n   113\t    }\n   114\t\n   115\t    private static String repeat(char c, int times) {\n   116\t        char[] out = new char[times];\n   117\t        java.util.Arrays.fill(out, c);\n   118\t        return new String(out);\n   119\t    }\n   120\t}\nFILE AttemptThrottle.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.ArrayDeque;\n     4\timport java.util.ArrayList;\n     5\timport java.util.List;\n     6\timport java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;\n     7\timport java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicLong;\n     8\t\n     9\t/**\n    10\t * Per-account lockout and per-source rate limiting, applied to the same attempt.\n    11\t *\n    12\t * <p><b>Two budgets, deliberately independent.</b> A single limit keyed on the account lets an\n    13\t * attacker who knows one username spend that account's whole budget and lock its real owner out; a\n    14\t * single limit keyed on the source lets an attacker with many usernames exhaust one shared NAT or\n    15\t * proxy address. Both are needed and neither may be reachable by the other's traffic:\n    16\t * <ul>\n    17\t *   <li>the per-account budget only ever counts failures against <em>that</em> account;</li>\n    18\t *   <li>the per-source window counts <em>all</em> attempts from that address and is configured, by\n    19\t *       {@link AuthConfig}, to be at least the account lock threshold, so one targeted account\n    20\t *       cannot on its own exhaust a shared address's budget. {@code sourceMaxAttempts} must be set\n    21\t *       well above {@code accountLockAfter} in any deployment that serves shared addresses - the\n    22\t *       README gives the sizing rule.</li>\n    23\t * </ul>\n    24\t *\n    25\t * <p><b>Check and act are one operation.</b> Every verdict is produced inside a single\n    26\t * {@link ConcurrentHashMap#compute(Object, java.util.function.BiFunction)} call, which the map runs\n    27\t * atomically for that key. There is no \"read the count, decide, then write it back\" window in which\n    28\t * two concurrent attempts could both be allowed, and no separate lock that could be held across the\n    29\t * credential check.\n    30\t *\n    31\t * <p><b>Bounded and fail-closed.</b> Both maps are capped. When a cap is reached the table is swept\n    32\t * for keys that have been idle longer than the longest window; if the table is still full the\n    33\t * verdict for a new key is {@code BLOCKED} rather than \"untracked\", because an untracked key is an\n    34\t * unlimited key. An attacker cannot grow this memory without bound by spraying usernames.\n    35\t *\n    36\t * <p>State is keyed on server-computed values only: the canonical username resolved from the store\n    37\t * (never the submitted spelling) and the canonical address produced by\n    38\t * {@link InputValidator#normalizeSourceAddress}.\n    39\t *\n    40\t * <p>Residual risk, stated in the README: this state is per process. Behind a load balancer the\n    41\t * budgets are per node, so the effective limit is the configured limit times the node count; a\n    42\t * deployment needing a shared budget must enforce it in a shared store, and this component's limits\n    43\t * remain as the last line of defence.\n    44\t */\n    45\tfinal class AttemptThrottle {\n    46\t\n    47\t    /** Cap on tracked account keys. */\n    48\t    static final int MAX_ACCOUNT_KEYS = 20_000;\n    49\t\n    50\t    /** Cap on tracked source keys. */\n    51\t    static final int MAX_SOURCE_KEYS = 20_000;\n    52\t\n    53\t    /** Operations between sweeps for idle keys. */\n    54\t    private static final int SWEEP_INTERVAL = 64;\n    55\t\n    56\t    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, AccountBucket> accounts = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    57\t    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, SourceBucket> sources = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    58\t    private final AtomicLong operations = new AtomicLong();\n    59\t\n    60\t    /** Verdict of a throttle check. */\n    61\t    enum Kind {\n    62\t\n    63\t        /** Proceed with the credential check. */\n    64\t        ALLOW,\n    65\t\n    66\t        /** Do not proceed; the budget for this key is exhausted. */\n    67\t        BLOCKED,\n    68\t\n    69\t        /** Do not proceed; the key's temporary lock is in force. */\n    70\t        LOCKED\n    71\t    }\n    72\t\n    73\t    /** Immutable verdict, carrying the retry hint when the answer is no. */\n    74\t    static final class Decision {\n    75\t\n    76\t        private final Kind kind;\n    77\t        private final int retryAfterSeconds;\n    78\t\n    79\t        Decision(Kind kind, int retryAfterSeconds) {\n    80\t            this.kind = kind;\n    81\t            this.retryAfterSeconds = retryAfterSeconds;\n    82\t        }\n    83\t\n    84\t        Kind kind() {\n    85\t            return kind;\n    86\t        }\n    87\t\n    88\t        int retryAfterSeconds() {\n    89\t            return retryAfterSeconds;\n    90\t        }\n    91\t\n    92\t        boolean allowed() {\n    93\t            return kind == Kind.ALLOW;\n    94\t        }\n    95\t\n    96\t        @Override\n    97\t        public String toString() {\n    98\t            return \"Decision[\" + kind + \", retryAfterSeconds=\" + retryAfterSeconds + \"]\";\n    99\t        }\n   100\t    }\n   101\t\n   102\t    private static final Decision ALLOW = new Decision(Kind.ALLOW, 0);\n   103\t\n   104\t    /**\n   105\t     * The throttle key for a submitted name: ASCII case folded, nothing else.\n   106\t     *\n   107\t     * <p>Keying on the <em>submitted</em> name rather than on a resolved record is deliberate. If\n   108\t     * the key existed only after a successful lookup, an attacker flooding names that do not exist\n   109\t     * would create no state at all and would face no per-name accounting; keying on the submitted\n   110\t     * name means the flood is accounted to the name it targets. static String canonicalize(String submitted) {\n   116\t        if (submitted == null) {\n   117\t            return \"\";\n   118\t        }\n   119\t        StringBuilder out = new 128\t        return out.toString();\n   129\t    }\n   130\t\n   131\t    /**\n   132\t     * Consumes one attempt from the source budget and applies its lock.\n   133\t     *\n   134\t     * <p>Called before any credential work, so an attacker cannot buy a full PBKDF2 round per\n   135\t     * attempt beyond their budget.\n   136\t     */\n   137\t    Decision consumeSourceAttempt(String windowSeconds * 1000L;\n   144\t            if (bucket == null) {\n   145\t                if (sources.size() >= MAX_SOURCE_KEYS && !evictIdleSources(horizon)) {\n   146\t                    observed.add(new Decision(Kind.BLOCKED, blockSeconds));\n   147\t                    return null;\n   148\t                }\n   149\t                bucket = new SourceBucket();\n   150\t            }\n   151\t            synchronized (bucket) {\n   152\t                bucket.lastTouchedMillis = nowMillis;\n   153\t                if (bucket.blockedUntilMillis > nowMillis) {\n   154\t                    observed.add(new Decision(Kind.BLOCKED, secondsUntil(bucket.blockedUntilMillis, nowMillis)));\n   155\t                    return bucket;\n   156\t                }\n   157\t }\n   173\t\n   174\t    /**\n   175\t     * Applies the per-account lock, without consuming anything.\n   176\t     *\n   177\t     * <p>Read-only with respect to the budget: an attacker cannot lengthen another party's lock, and\n   178\t     * probing a locked account does not extend the lock either.\n   179\t     */\n   180\t    Decision checkAccountLock(String canonicalUsername, long blockedForever() : observed.get(0);\n   228\t    }\n   229\t\n   230\t    /**\n   231\t     * Clears an account's failure state after a verified sign-in.\n   232\t     *\n   233\t     * <p>Clearing on success rather than on window expiry is what stops a lockout from being a\n   234\t     * permanent handle: an attacker cannot keep an account locked by generating failures around the\n   235\t     * owner's successful sign-ins, because the owner's success resets the streak.\n   236\t     */\n   237\t    void resetAccount(String canonicalUsername) {\n   238\t        accounts.computeIfPresent(canonicalUsername, (key, bucket) -> {\n   239\t            synchronized (bucket) {\n   240\t                bucket.consecutiveFailures = 0;\n   241\t                bucket.blockedUntilMillis = 0L;\n   242\t            }\n   243\t            return bucket;\n   244\t        });\n   245\t    }\n   246\t\n   247\t    /** Number of keys currently tracked; used by the deployment check and by tests. */\n   248\t    int trackedAccountKeys() {\n   249\t        return accounts.size();\n   250\t    }\n   251\t\n   252\t    int trackedSourceKeys() {\n   253\t        return sources.size();\n   254\t    }\n   255\t\n   256\t    /**\n   257\t     * Drops keys idle longer than the sweep horizon.\n   258\t     *\n   259\t     * <p>The clock passed in is the same one every verdict is computed from. Reading\n   260\t     * {@link System#currentTimeMillis()} here instead would compare the injected timeline against\n   261\t     * the wall clock: wherever the two differ - a test clock, or a deployment whose clock was stepped\n   262\t     * after buckets were created - the sweep would see every bucket as idle and delete the lockout\n   263\t     * state while authentication kept reporting healthy limits.\n   264\t     */\n   265\t    private void sweepIfDue(long nowMillis) {\n   266\t        long tick = operations.incrementAndGet();\n   267\t        if (tick % SWEEP_INTERVAL != 0) {\n   268\t            return;\n   269\t        }\n   270\t        long idleHorizon = nowMillis - SWEEP_INTERVAL_IDLE_MILLIS;\n   271\t        accounts.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> {\n   272\t            AccountBucket bucket = entry.getValue();\n   273\t            synchronized (bucket) {\n   274\t                return bucket.lastTouchedMillis < idleHorizon && bucket.blockedUntilMillis <= nowMillis;\n   275\t            }\n   276\t        });\n   277\t        sources.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> {\n   278\t            SourceBucket bucket = entry.getValue();\n   279\t            synchronized (bucket) {\n   280\t                // Stamps older than the longest window the configuration accepts can no longer\n   281\t                // affect any verdict, so they are dropped here. Refusing is the only\n   327\t     * fail-closed answer: reporting \"allowed\" for an untracked key would make the cap a way to\n   328\t     * obtain unlimited attempts.\n   329\t     */\n   330\t    private static Decision blockedForever() {\n   331\t        return new Decision(Kind.BLOCKED, MAX_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS);\n   332\t    }\n   333\t\n   334\t    /** Upper bound on the advertised retry hint, so it stays usable as a {@code Retry-After}. */\n   335\t    static final int MAX_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS = 3600;\n   336\t\n   337\t    private static int secondsUntil(long untilMillis, long nowMillis) {\n   338\t        long delta = untilMillis - nowMillis;\n   339\t        if (delta <= 0) {\n   340\t            return 1;\n   341\t        }\n   342\t        long seconds = (delta + 999L) / 1000L;\n   343\t        return (int) Math.min(seconds, MAX_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS);\n   344\t    }\n   345\t\n   346\t    private static final class AccountBucket {\n   347\t\n   348\t        private int consecutiveFailures;\n   349\t        private long blockedUntilMillis;\n   350\t        private long lastTouchedMillis = System.currentTimeMillis();\n   351\t\n   352\t        private AccountBucket() {\n   353\t        }\n   354\t    }\n   355\t\n   356\t    private static final class SourceBucket {\n   357\t\n   358\t        private final ArrayDeque<Long> attempts = new ArrayDeque<>();\n   359\t        private long blockedUntilMillis;\n   360\t        private long lastTouchedMillis = System.currentTimeMillis();\n   361\t\n   362\t        private SourceBucket() {\n   363\t        }\n   364\t\n   365\t        /** Drops stamps that no legal window could still count. Callers hold the bucket monitor. */\n   366\t        private void pruneStampsBefore(long horizon) {\n   367\t            while (!attempts.isEmpty() && attempts.peekFirst() < horizon) {\n   368\t                attempts.pollFirst();\n   369\t            }\n   370\t        }\n   371\t    }\n   372\t}\nFILE AuditLogger.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.io.IOException;\n     4\timport java.nio.ByteBuffer;\n     5\timport java.nio.channels.FileChannel;\n     6\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     7\timport java.nio.file.Files;\n     8\timport java.nio.file.LinkOption;\n     9\timport java.nio.file.OpenOption;\n    10\timport java.nio.file.Path;\n    11\timport java.nio.file.StandardOpenOption;\n    12\timport java.nio.file.attribute.FileAttribute;\n    13\timport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermissions;\n    14\timport java.util.ArrayDeque;\n    15\timport java.util.ArrayList;\n    16\timport java.util.Collections;\n    17\timport java.util.Deque;\n    18\timport java.util.EnumSet;\n    19\timport java.util.List;\n    20\timport java.util.Set;\n    21\t\n    22\t/**\n    23\t * Append-only authentication event log.\n    24\t *\n    25\t * <p><b>Fail-closed by construction.</b> The factory will not return an instance that silently\n    26\t * discards events, and {@link #record(AuthEvent)} verifies the write: if the channel reports fewer\n    27\t * bytes than the encoder produced, or the rendered line would be unsafe to write, it throws\n    28\t * {@link AuditPersistenceException} and {@link AuthenticationService} refuses the attempt.\n    29\t * Authentication therefore cannot proceed with an unrecorded event.\n    30\t *\n    31\t * <p>Nothing latches. A transient condition (a full file system, a brief write error) refuses the\n    32\t * attempts that hit it; once the condition clears, the next attempt authenticates again. Free space\n    33\t * is re-checked at most once every {@value #FREE_SPACE_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MILLIS} ms and the interval\n    34\t * is re-armed after every failed write, so recovery is automatic and does not need an operator.\n    35\t *\n    36\t * <p>File handling (all mode checks go through {@link PosixMode} and therefore fail closed):\n    37\t * <ul>\n    38\t *   <li>the parent directory must exist and must not be writable by group or other users - a\n    39\t *       writable directory lets another user replace, delete or pre-create the log at that name;</li>\n    40\t *   <li>an existing log file must be a regular file (checked without following symlinks), readable,\n    41\t *       writable, and exactly owner-only ({@code 0600});</li>\n    42\t *   <li>a new log file is created with the owner-only permission set (equivalent to {@code 0600})\n    43\t *       using {@code CREATE_NEW}, so anything already present at that name - including a symlink -\n    44\t *       makes creation fail rather than being adopted; the resulting permissions are read back and\n    45\t *       verified;</li>\n    46\t *   <li>a file whose last record is truncated - the signature of a process that died, or a write\n    47\t *       that returned short, mid-line - is repaired before anything is appended to it, see\n    48\t *       {@link #discardTornTail(Path)};</li>\n    49\t *   <li>the file system must have at least the configured free-space headroom, and the file itself\n    50\t *       must be under {@value #MAX_LOG_BYTES} bytes.</li>\n    51\t * </ul>\n    52\t *\n    53\t * <p>Retention: the last {@code maxEvents} rendered lines are kept in a bounded in-memory ring so\n    54\t * recent activity can be reviewed without unbounded growth. The file is the durable record; rotation\n    55\t * and long-term storage are operator concerns and are noted in the README.\n    56\t */\n    57\tfinal class AuditLogger {\n    58\t\n    59\t    /**\n    60\t     * Ceiling on the log file, enforced at open and before every write. The same bound\n    61\t     * {@code AuthConfig} applies to a pre-existing file at start-up; without the per-write half, a\n    62\t     * long-lived process would push the file past that bound and every restart would then refuse to\n    63\t     * boot, so the operator's only recovery window would be before the first restart.\n    64\t     */\n    65\t    static final long MAX_LOG_BYTES = 64L * 1024L * 1024L;\n    66\t\n    67\t    /** Bytes read per step when scanning backwards for the last record boundary. */\n    68\t    private static final int TAIL_SCAN_CHUNK = 8 * 1024;\n    69\t\n    70\t    /** Upper bound on what {@link #readBack()} will read, so review cannot be made to thrash the disk. */\n    71\t    private static final long MAX_READ_BACK_BYTES = 4L * 1024L * 1024L;\n    72\t\n    73\t    private static final long FREE_SPACE_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MILLIS = 30_000L;\n    74\t\n    75\t    private final Path file;\n    76\t    private final int maxEvents;\n    77\t    private final long minFreeBytes;\n    78\t    private final long maxLogBytes;\n    79\t\n    80\t    private final Deque<String> retained = new ArrayDeque<>();\n    81\t    private FileChannel channel;\n    82\t    /**\n    83\t     * Identity ({@code dev}+{@code ino}) of the inode {@link #channel} was opened against. Compared\n    84\t     * before every write, see {@link #targetStillOurs()}.\n    85\t     */\n    86\t    private Object openedFileKey;\n    87\t    private long sequence;\n    88\t    private long totalRecorded;\n    89\t    private long unsavedEvents;\n    90\t    /**\n    91\t     * Lifetime total of bytes discarded while repairing torn tails - what {@link #discardedTornBytes()}\n    92\t     * reports.\n    93\t     */\n    94\t    private long discardedTornBytes;\n    95\t    /** Bytes discarded since the last {@code log_partial_record} alert, so a repair is reported once. */\n    96\t    private long pendingTornAlertBytes;\n    97\t    private long lastFreeSpaceCheckMillis;\n    98\t    private long lastWriteFailureMillis;\n    99\t    private boolean closed;\n   100\t    private boolean alertInFlight;\n   101\t\n   102\t    private AuditLogger(Path file, int maxEvents, long minFreeBytes, long maxLogBytes, long nowMillis) {\n   103\t        this.file = file;\n   104\t        this.maxEvents = maxEvents;\n   105\t        this.minFreeBytes = minFreeBytes;\n   106\t        this.maxLogBytes = maxLogBytes;\n   107\t        this.lastFreeSpaceCheckMillis = nowMillis;\n   108\t    }\n   109\t\n   110\t    /**\n   111\t     * Opens or creates the log file.\n   112\t     *\n   113\t     * @throws InvalidConfigException when the location or the permissions would leave the log unsafe\n   114\t     *     or unwritable. Problems name the condition only, never a client-supplied value.\n   115\t     */\n   116\t    static AuditLogger open(Path file, int maxEvents, long minFreeBytes, long nowMillis) throws InvalidConfigException {\n   117\t        return open(file, maxEvents, minFreeBytes, MAX_LOG_BYTES, nowMillis);\n   118\t    }\n   119\t\n   120\t    /**\n   121\t     * As {@link #open(Path, int, long, long)} with the size ceiling supplied, so the per-write bound\n   122\t     * in {@link #storageProblem()} can be exercised at a scale a test can reach. The production\n   123\t     * overload above is the only one the service calls.\n   124\t     */\n   125\t    static AuditLogger open(Path file, int maxEvents, long minFreeBytes, long maxLogBytes, long nowMillis)\n   126\t            throws InvalidConfigException {\n   127\t        List<String> problems = new ArrayList<>();\n   128\t        if (file == null || maxEvents < 1 || maxLogBytes < 1L) {\n   129\t            problems.add(\"event log configuration is incomplete\");\n   130\t            throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   131\t        }\n   132\t        Path parent = file.getParent();\n   133\t        if (parent == null || !Files.isDirectory(parent, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n   134\t            problems.add(\"event log directory does not exist\");\n   135\t            throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   136\t        }\n   137\t        if (PosixMode.writableByGroupOrOther(parent)) {\n   138\t            problems.add(\"event log directory is writable by group or other users, \"\n   139\t                    + \"or its permissions could not be verified\");\n   140\t            throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   141\t        }\n   142\t        boolean exists = Files.exists(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n   143\t        if (exists) {\n   144\t            if (!Files.isRegularFile(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n   145\t                problems.add(\"event log path exists but is not a regular file\");\n   146\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   147\t            }\n   148\t            if (!Files.isWritable(file)) {\n   149\t                problems.add(\"event log file is not writable\");\n   150\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   151\t            }\n   152\t        } else {\n   153\t            try {\n   154\t                // CREATE_NEW rather than CREATE+APPEND: anything already at the target name,\n   155\t                // including a symlink another user placed there, must fail the open rather than be\n   156\t                // adopted and written through.\n   157\t                Set<? extends OpenOption> createOptions = EnumSet.of(StandardOpenOption.CREATE_NEW, StandardOpenOption.WRITE);\n   158\t                // Created empty and closed immediately: the point is the create with the owner-only\n   159\t                // mode, not a handle to keep.\n   160\t                FileChannel created = FileChannel.open(file, createOptions, ownerOnlyAttributes());\n   161\t                created.close();\n   162\t            } catch (IOException e) {\n   163\t                problems.add(\"event log file could not be created\");\n   164\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   165\t            }\n   166\t        }\n   167\t        if (PosixMode.notExactlyOwnerOnly(file)) {\n   168\t            problems.add(\"event log file is not exactly owner-only (0600), or its permissions could \"\n   169\t                    + \"not be verified\");\n   170\t        }\n   171\t        if (!problems.isEmpty()) {\n   172\t            throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   173\t        }\n   174\t        long discarded = discardTornTail(file);\n   175\t        if (discarded < 0L) {\n   176\t            problems.add(\"the event log file ends in a partial record that could not be repaired\");\n   177\t            throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   178\t        }\n   179\t        AuditLogger logger = new AuditLogger(file, maxEvents, minFreeBytes, maxLogBytes, nowMillis);\n   180\t        logger.discardedTornBytes = discarded;\n   181\t        logger.pendingTornAlertBytes = discarded;\n   182\t        logger.checkFreeSpace(problems);\n   183\t        if (!problems.isEmpty()) {\n   184\t            throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   185\t        }\n   186\t        try {\n   187\t            logger.channel = FileChannel.open(file, StandardOpenOption.WRITE, StandardOpenOption.APPEND);\n   188\t            logger.openedFileKey = fileKeyOf(file);\n   189\t        } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n   190\t            problems.add(\"event log file could not be opened for append\");\n   191\t            throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   192\t        }\n   193\t        if (discarded > 0L) {\n   194\t            // The repair removes evidence, so it is itself an event. Written after the channel is\n   195\t            // open, and never in place of the events it made room for. If even that write fails the\n   196\t            // pending count survives, so the next successful write reports the gap.\n   197\t            if (logger.recordAlert(Diagnostic.LOG_PARTIAL_RECORD, (String) null, null)) {\n   198\t                logger.pendingTornAlertBytes = 0L;\n   199\t            }\n   200\t        }\n   201\t        return logger;\n   202\t    }\n   203\t\n   204\t    /**\n   205\t     * Writes one event and the retained copy.\n   206\t     *\n   207\t     * @return the line that was persisted\n   208\t     * @throws AuditPersistenceException when the event could not be persisted. The caller must treat\n   209\t     *     this as a security failure and refuse the attempt rather than proceed.\n   210\t     */\n   211\t    synchronized String record(AuthEvent event) throws AuditPersistenceException {\n   212\t        if (closed) {\n   213\t            throw new AuditPersistenceException(Diagnostic.AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE);\n   214\t        }\n   215\t        String line = event == null ? null : event.renderOrReject(sequence + 1);\n   216\t        if (line == null) {\n   217\t            // An unsafe line is a defect in this process, not a client problem. The unsafe content\n   218\t            // is never written; the caller records an alert naming the reason instead.\n   219\t            unsavedEvents++;\n   220\t            throw new AuditPersistenceException(Diagnostic.LOG_LINE_REJECTED, event);\n   221\t        }\n   222\t        if (channel == null) {\n   223\t            reopenIfPossible();\n   224\t            if (channel == null) {\n   225\t                unsavedEvents++;\n   226\t                throw new AuditPersistenceException(Diagnostic.LOG_WRITE_FAILED, event);\n   227\t            }\n   228\t            if (pendingTornAlertBytes > 0L) {\n   229\t                // Discarding a torn tail removes evidence, so the next event reports the gap instead\n   230\t                // of leaving the file silently short. recordAlert is re-entrant on this monitor and\n   231\t                // cannot recurse back into record().\n   232\t                if (recordAlert(Diagnostic.LOG_PARTIAL_RECORD, (String) null, null)) {\n   233\t                    pendingTornAlertBytes = 0L;\n   234\t                }\n   235\t            }\n   236\t        }\n   237\t        if (!targetStillOurs()) {\n   238\t            unsavedEvents++;\n   239\t            throw new AuditPersistenceException(Diagnostic.LOG_WRITE_FAILED, event);\n   240\t        }\n   241\t        Diagnostic storageProblem = storageProblem();\n   242\t        if (storageProblem != null) {\n   243\t            unsavedEvents++;\n   244\t            throw new AuditPersistenceException(storageProblem, event);\n   245\t        }\n   246\t        writeAndRetain(line, event);\n   247\t        return line;\n   248\t    }\n   249\t\n   250\t    /**\n   251\t     * Writes an alert about a failure, without recursing if that write also fails.\n   252\t     *\n   253\t     * @return {@code true} when the alert itself was persisted. When it is {@code false} the\n   254\t     *     condition is unrecoverable from here and the caller must keep refusing.\n   255\t     */\n   256\t    synchronized boolean recordAlert(Diagnostic reason, String submittedUsername, String canonicalSource) {\n   257\t        AuthEvent fallback = AuthEvent.builder()\n   258\t                .timestampMillis(System.currentTimeMillis())\n   259\t                .account(submittedUsername, false)\n   260\t                .source(canonicalSource)\n   261\t                .latencyMicros(0L)\n   262\t                .build();\n   263\t        return recordAlert(reason, fallback);\n   264\t    }\n   265\t\n   266\t    /**\n   267\t     * Writes an alert about {@code original}, keeping whatever of it was already safe to record.\n   268\t     *\n   269\t     * <p>The alert exists because one event could not be written, so the fields of that event are the\n   270\t     * evidence most worth keeping. Re-deriving them from the caller's arguments instead would report\n   271\t     * every throttled, wrong-account and locked-out attempt as though it named no account at all,\n   272\t     * which is precisely the attribution a reviewer needs. Only fields this class would itself have\n   273\t     * written are carried over - an alert about a rejected line must not try to write it again.\n   274\t     */\n   275\t    synchronized boolean recordAlert(Diagnostic reason, AuthEvent original) {\n   276\t        if (closed || alertInFlight) {\n   277\t            unsavedEvents++;\n   278\t            return false;\n   279\t        }\n   280\t        alertInFlight = true;\n   281\t        try {\n   282\t            if (channel == null) {\n   283\t                reopenIfPossible();\n   284\t                if (channel == null) {\n   285\t                    unsavedEvents++;\n   286\t                    return false;\n   287\t                }\n   288\t            }\n   289\t            if (!targetStillOurs()) {\n   290\t                unsavedEvents++;\n   291\t                return false;\n   292\t            }\n   293\t            AuthEvent alert = AuthEvent.builder()\n   294\t                    .timestampMillis(original == null ? System.currentTimeMillis() : original.timestampMillis())\n   295\t                    .type(EventType.SECURITY_ALERT)\n   296\t                    .outcome(\"error\")\n   297\t                    .accountFieldOf(original == null ? null : original.accountField())\n   298\t                    .source(original == null ? null : original.sourceIfSafe())\n   299\t                    .reason(reason)\n   300\t                    .latencyMicros(original == null ? 0L : original.latencyMicrosIfSafe())\n   301\t                    .retryAfterSeconds(original == null ? 0 : original.retryAfterSecondsIfSafe())\n   302\t                    .build();\n   303\t            String line = alert.renderOrReject(sequence + 1);\n   304\t            if (line == null) {\n   305\t                unsavedEvents++;\n   306\t                return false;\n   307\t            }\n   308\t            writeAndRetain(line, alert);\n   309\t            return true;\n   310\t        } catch (AuditPersistenceException e) {\n   311\t            // writeAndRetain already counted the hole, stamped the failure, re-armed the free-space\n   312\t            // check and closed the channel.\n   313\t            return false;\n   314\t        } catch (RuntimeException e) {\n   315\t            lastWriteFailureMillis = System.currentTimeMillis();\n   316\t            lastFreeSpaceCheckMillis = 0L;\n   317\t            closeChannelQuietly();\n   318\t            unsavedEvents++;\n   319\t            return false;\n   320\t        } finally {\n   321\t            alertInFlight = false;\n   322\t        }\n   323\t    }\n   324\t\n   325\t    /**\n   326\t     * Appends one already-rendered line, verifies that every byte landed, and only then counts the\n   327\t     * line as recorded.\n   328\t     *\n   329\t     * <p>Both writers go through here so that neither can forget part of the bookkeeping. A failed\n   330\t     * write closes the channel (so the next attempt re-validates the target instead of appending\n   331\t     * blind into an inode we no longer trust), stamps the failure, and resets the free-space interval\n   332\t     * - the claim in the class note above that space is re-checked after a failed write is this line,\n   333\t     * not an assumption.\n   334\t     *\n   335\t     * <p>A short write is not retried against the same channel. The bytes already in the file are a\n   336\t     * partial record, and the only sound response is to stop writing and let the next attempt\n   337\t     * re-open, at which point {@link #discardTornTail(Path)} removes the partial record before\n   338\t     * anything else is appended. {@code APPEND} alone would otherwise splice the next event onto the\n   339\t     * fragment and produce one line that is a mixture of two attempts.\n   340\t     */\n   341\t    private void writeAndRetain(String line, AuthEvent event) throws AuditPersistenceException {\n   342\t        byte[] bytes = (line + \"\\n\").getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n   343\t        try {\n   344\t            ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.wrap(bytes);\n   345\t            while (buffer.hasRemaining()) {\n   346\t                if (channel.write(buffer) <= 0) {\n   347\t                    throw new IOException(\"the log channel accepted no further bytes\");\n   348\t                }\n   349\t            }\n   350\t            channel.force(false);\n   351\t        } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n   352\t            unsavedEvents++;\n   353\t            lastWriteFailureMillis = System.currentTimeMillis();\n   354\t            lastFreeSpaceCheckMillis = 0L;\n   355\t            closeChannelQuietly();\n   356\t            throw new AuditPersistenceException(Diagnostic.LOG_WRITE_FAILED, event);\n   357\t        }\n   358\t        sequence++;\n   359\t        totalRecorded++;\n   360\t        retained.addLast(line);\n   361\t        while (retained.size() > maxEvents) {\n   362\t            retained.removeFirst();\n   363\t        }\n   364\t    }\n   365\t\n   366\t    /** Lines currently retained in memory, oldest first. Never contains credentials. */\n   367\t    synchronized List<String> retained() {\n   368\t        return Collections.unmodifiableList(new ArrayList<>(retained));\n   369\t    }\n   370\t\n   371\t    synchronized long totalRecorded() {\n   372\t        return totalRecorded;\n   373\t    }\n   374\t\n   375\t    /**\n   376\t     * Events this process tried to write and could not. Every one of those cost the caller a denial,\n   377\t     * so the number is the size of the hole in the audit trail: without it an operator sees a healthy\n   378\t     * service producing an empty log, and nobody can tell \"no activity\" from \"activity refused\n   379\t     * because activity cannot be recorded\".\n   380\t     */\n   381\t    synchronized long unsavedEvents() {\n   382\t        return unsavedEvents;\n   383\t    }\n   384\t\n   385\t    /**\n   386\t     * Bytes of a partial trailing record this process discarded while repairing the log. Not counted\n   387\t     * in {@link #unsavedEvents()} - those bytes were never written by this process - but the hole is\n   388\t     * real, which is why a repair is itself logged as an alert.\n   389\t     */\n   390\t    synchronized long discardedTornBytes() {\n   391\t        return discardedTornBytes;\n   392\t    }\n   393\t\n   394\t    /** {@code true} when the most recent write attempt failed; never used to block forever. */\n   395\t    synchronized boolean lastWriteFailed() {\n   396\t        return lastWriteFailureMillis != 0 && channel == null;\n   397\t    }\n   398\t\n   399\t    synchronized boolean isClosed() {\n   400\t        return closed;\n   401\t    }\n   402\t\n   403\t    Path file() {\n   404\t        return file;\n   405\t    }\n   406\t\n   407\t    /**\n   408\t     * Reads the tail of the file back from disk; used by the deployment check and by tests.\n   409\t     *\n   410\t     * <p>Bounded to the last {@value #MAX_READ_BACK_BYTES} bytes: the log may legitimately sit at its\n   411\t     * size ceiling, and a review helper that loads a whole audit file into memory turns a routine\n   412\t     * status call into a disk-and-heap incident. A leading partial line (the read starting mid-record)\n   413\t     * is dropped rather than reported as a record.\n   414\t     */\n   415\t    List<String> readBack() throws IOException {\n   416\t        if (!Files.isRegularFile(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n   417\t            return Collections.emptyList();\n   418\t        }\n   419\t        long size = Files.size(file);\n   420\t        if (size == 0L) {\n   421\t            return Collections.emptyList();\n   422\t        }\n   423\t        long skip = Math.max(0L, size - MAX_READ_BACK_BYTES);\n   424\t        int length = (int) (size - skip);\n   425\t        byte[] raw = new byte[length];\n   426\t        try (FileChannel probe = FileChannel.open(file, StandardOpenOption.READ)) {\n   427\t            ByteBuffer target = ByteBuffer.wrap(raw);\n   428\t            long position = skip;\n   429\t            int stalls = 0;\n   430\t            while (target.hasRemaining()) {\n   431\t                if (probe.read(target, position) < 0L && ++stalls > 8) {\n   432\t                    throw new IOException(\"the event log ended before the bytes it reported\");\n   433\t                }\n   434\t                position = size - target.remaining();\n   435\t            }\n   436\t        }\n   437\t        String text = new String(raw, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n   438\t        List<String> lines = new ArrayList<>();\n   439\t        int from = skip > 0L ? text.indexOf('\\n') + 1 : 0;\n   440\t        int at = from;\n   441\t        while (at < text.length()) {\n   442\t            int nl = text.indexOf('\\n', at);\n   443\t            int end = nl < 0 ? text.length() : nl;\n   444\t            if (end > at) {\n   445\t                lines.add(text.substring(at, end));\n   446\t            }\n   447\t            if (nl < 0) {\n   448\t                break;\n   449\t            }\n   450\t            at = nl + 1;\n   451\t        }\n   452\t        return lines;\n   453\t    }\n   454\t\n   455\t    synchronized void close() {\n   456\t        if (closed) {\n   457\t            return;\n   458\t        }\n   459\t        closed = true;\n   460\t        closeChannelQuietly();\n   461\t    }\n   462\t\n   463\t    private boolean freeSpaceCheckDue() {\n   464\t        long now = System.currentTimeMillis();\n   465\t        if (now - lastFreeSpaceCheckMillis < FREE_SPACE_RECHECK_INTERVAL_MILLIS) {\n   466\t            return false;\n   467\t        }\n   468\t        lastFreeSpaceCheckMillis = now;\n   469\t        return true;\n   470\t    }\n   471\t\n   472\t    /**\n   473\t     * Checks what the log can still absorb before a write is attempted.\n   474\t     *\n   475\t     * @return the reason to refuse, or {@code null} when the write may proceed\n   476\t     */\n   477\t    private Diagnostic storageProblem() {\n   478\t        if (freeSpaceCheckDue()) {\n   479\t            List<String> problems = new ArrayList<>();\n   480\t            checkFreeSpace(problems);\n   481\t            if (!problems.isEmpty()) {\n   482\t                return Diagnostic.LOG_STORAGE_FULL;\n   483\t            }\n   484\t        }\n   485\t        long size;\n   486\t        try {\n   487\t            size = Files.size(file);\n   488\t        } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n   489\t            // The file we were appending to cannot be sized: treat it as untrusted rather than\n   490\t            // write blind.\n   491\t            return Diagnostic.LOG_WRITE_FAILED;\n   492\t        }\n   493\t        if (size >= maxLogBytes) {\n   494\t            return Diagnostic.LOG_FILE_LIMIT;\n   495\t        }\n   496\t        return null;\n   497\t    }\n   498\t\n   499\t    /** Re-opens a channel that a failed write closed, so a transient error does not persist. */\n   500\t    private void reopenIfPossible() {\n   501\t        if (closed || channel != null) {\n   502\t            return;\n   503\t        }\n   504\t        try {\n   505\t            if (!Files.isRegularFile(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n   506\t                return;\n   507\t            }\n   508\t            if (PosixMode.notExactlyOwnerOnly(file)) {\n   509\t                return;\n   510\t            }\n   511\t            long discarded = discardTornTail(file);\n   512\t            if (discarded < 0L) {\n   513\t                // The tail cannot be repaired, so appending would build a corrupted record on top of\n   514\t                // it. Refuse and keep refusing until the file is dealt with out of band.\n   515\t                return;\n   516\t            }\n   517\t            discardedTornBytes += discarded;\n   518\t            pendingTornAlertBytes += discarded;\n   519\t            FileChannel reopened = FileChannel.open(file,\n   520\t                    EnumSet.of(StandardOpenOption.WRITE, StandardOpenOption.APPEND));\n   521\t            channel = reopened;\n   522\t            openedFileKey = fileKeyOf(file);\n   523\t            lastWriteFailureMillis = 0L;\n   524\t        } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n   525\t            channel = null;\n   526\t            openedFileKey = null;\n   527\t        }\n   528\t    }\n   529\t\n   530\t    /**\n   531\t     * Confirms that the name this logger writes to still denotes the file it was opened against, and\n   532\t     * is still safe to write.\n   533\t     *\n   534\t     * <p>An open {@link FileChannel} keeps writing into an inode even after some other process\n   535\t     * deletes or replaces the path that named it. Without this check the service would keep\n   536\t     * authenticating while every event went to an unlinked inode that vanishes on close: the audit\n   537\t     * trail stops existing and nothing reports it. The same check refuses a path that became a\n   538\t     * symlink, one replaced by a file another user can read or write, and one that was deleted.\n   539\t     *\n   540\t     * <p>Runs on every write rather than on a timer. A single unverified write is enough to lose an\n   541\t     * event, and one {@code getattr} is nothing next to the key derivation every attempt already\n   542\t     * performs.\n   543\t     *\n   544\t     * @return {@code false} when the write must not proceed; the channel is closed so the next\n   545\t     *     attempt can try to reopen, which keeps recovery automatic\n   546\t     */\n   547\t    private boolean targetStillOurs() {\n   548\t        final java.nio.file.attribute.BasicFileAttributes attrs;\n   549\t        try {\n   550\t            attrs = Files.readAttributes(file, java.nio.file.attribute.BasicFileAttributes.class,\n   551\t                    LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n   552\t        } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n   553\t            // Includes NoSuchFileException: the log name is gone, so the channel can only be\n   554\t            // writing into an inode nobody will ever read again.\n   555\t            closeChannelQuietly();\n   556\t            return false;\n   557\t        }\n   558\t        if (!attrs.isRegularFile()) {\n   559\t            closeChannelQuietly();\n   560\t            return false;\n   561\t        }\n   562\t        Object now = attrs.fileKey();\n   563\t        if (now != null && openedFileKey != null && !now.equals(openedFileKey)) {\n   564\t            closeChannelQuietly();\n   565\t            return false;\n   566\t        }\n   567\t        if (PosixMode.notExactlyOwnerOnly(file)) {\n   568\t            closeChannelQuietly();\n   569\t            return false;\n   570\t        }\n   571\t        return true;\n   572\t    }\n   573\t\n   574\t    private static Object fileKeyOf(Path path) {\n   575\t        try {\n   576\t            return Files.readAttributes(path, java.nio.file.attribute.BasicFileAttributes.class,\n   577\t                    LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS).fileKey();\n   578\t        } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n   579\t            return null;\n   580\t        }\n   581\t    }\n   582\t\n   583\t    /**\n   584\t     * Removes a partial record at the end of the log, so the next append starts on a real boundary.\n   585\t     *\n   586\t     * <p>Two things produce a file that does not end in a newline: a process killed mid-write, and a\n   587\t     * write whose bytes were only partly accepted. Both leave a fragment with no complete record in\n   588\t     * it, and because the log is opened with {@code APPEND}, nothing in the normal write path would\n   589\t     * ever notice - the next event is glued to the fragment and the two become one line. A reviewer\n   590\t     * reading that line sees a mixture of two attempts: the timestamp of one, the account of another.\n   591\t     * An audit trail that can silently merge records is worse than one with a visible hole in it.\n   592\t     *\n   593\t     * <p>The repair truncates to the byte after the last newline (to {@code 0} when the file holds no\n   594\t     * newline at all) and verifies the resulting size. The discarded bytes are returned so the caller\n   595\t     * can report the gap; every one of them belonged to a record that was already unreadable as a\n   596\t     * whole line, so nothing complete is ever removed.\n   597\t     *\n   598\t     * @return bytes discarded, {@code 0} when the file already ends on a boundary, or {@code -1}\n   599\t     *     when the file could not be examined or repaired\n   600\t     */\n   601\t    private static long discardTornTail(Path file) {\n   602\t        try (FileChannel probe = FileChannel.open(file,\n   603\t                EnumSet.of(StandardOpenOption.READ, StandardOpenOption.WRITE))) {\n   604\t            long size = probe.size();\n   605\t            if (size == 0L) {\n   606\t                return 0L;\n   607\t            }\n   608\t            long boundary = offsetAfterLastNewline(probe, size);\n   609\t            if (boundary == size) {\n   610\t                return 0L;\n   611\t            }\n   612\t            probe.truncate(boundary);\n   613\t            probe.force(false);\n   614\t            if (probe.size() != boundary) {\n   615\t                return -1L;\n   616\t            }\n   617\t            return size - boundary;\n   618\t        } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n   619\t            return -1L;\n   620\t        }\n   621\t    }\n   622\t\n   623\t    /**\n   624\t     * Scans backwards for the last newline, in bounded chunks, and returns the offset just past it\n   625\t     * (or {@code 0} when the file contains none). Reads only as far back as it must: a log at its\n   626\t     * size ceiling that ends correctly costs one 8 KiB read.\n   627\t     */\n   628\t    private static long offsetAfterLastNewline(FileChannel probe, long size) throws IOException {\n   629\t        byte[] chunk = new byte[TAIL_SCAN_CHUNK];\n   630\t        long end = size;\n   631\t        while (end > 0L) {\n   632\t            long start = Math.max(0L, end - chunk.length);\n   633\t            int length = (int) (end - start);\n   634\t            ByteBuffer buffer = ByteBuffer.wrap(chunk, 0, length);\n   635\t            int guard = 0;\n   636\t            while (buffer.hasRemaining()) {\n   637\t                if (probe.read(buffer, start) < 0L && ++guard > 8) {\n   638\t                    throw new IOException(\"the event log could not be read for the tail scan\");\n   639\t                }\n   640\t            }\n   641\t            for (int i = length - 1; i >= 0; i--) {\n   642\t                if (chunk[i] == '\\n') {\n   643\t                    return start + i + 1L;\n   644\t                }\n   645\t            }\n   646\t            end = start;\n   647\t        }\n   648\t        return 0L;\n   649\t    }\n   650\t\n   651\t    private void closeChannelQuietly() {\n   652\t        openedFileKey = null;\n   653\t        if (channel == null) {\n   654\t            return;\n   655\t        }\n   656\t        try {\n   657\t            channel.close();\n   658\t        } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n   659\t            // Nothing useful to do; the channel is abandoned and reopenIfPossible will retry.\n   660\t        }\n   661\t        channel = null;\n   662\t    }\n   663\t\n   664\t    private void checkFreeSpace(List<String> problems) {\n   665\t        Path parent = file.getParent();\n   666\t        if (parent == null) {\n   667\t            return;\n   668\t        }\n   669\t        long usable;\n   670\t        try {\n   671\t            usable = Files.getFileStore(parent).getUsableSpace();\n   672\t        } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n   673\t            problems.add(\"free space on the event log file system could not be determined\");\n   674\t            return;\n   675\t        }\n   676\t        if (usable < minFreeBytes) {\n   677\t            problems.add(\"the event log file system is below the configured free-space floor\");\n   678\t        }\n   679\t    }\n   680\t\n   681\t    private static FileAttribute<?>[] ownerOnlyAttributes() {\n   682\t        try {\n   683\t            return new FileAttribute<?>[] {PosixFilePermissions.asFileAttribute(PosixMode.OWNER_ONLY)};\n   684\t        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException e) {\n   685\t            return new FileAttribute<?>[0];\n   686\t        }\n   687\t    }\n   688\t}\nFILE AuditPersistenceException.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * Raised when an event could not be persisted.\n     5\t *\n     6\t * <p>The caller must treat authentication as failed. An event that is not durable is an\n     7\t * authentication decision that cannot be reviewed later, which is exactly the condition the log\n     8\t * exists to prevent, so the attempt is refused rather than allowed.\n     9\t *\n    10\t * <p>Like {@link AuthException}, this carries only a {@link Diagnostic} category. The value itself is never returned to callers of\n   442\t     * configuration code, never compared against anything printable, and never logged; only the\n   443\t     * success of the read is reported.\n   444\t     */\n   445\t    private static boolean passwordEntryIsReadable(Path keystore, char[] password, String alias, List<String> problems) {\n   446\t        // The type must come from the same rule the runtime reader uses. Probing a .jceks file as\n   447\t        // PKCS12 makes every deployment unbootable while the file itself is correct.\n   448\t        String type = KeystorePepperProvider.keystoreType(keystore);\n   449\t        if (type == null) {\n   450\t            problems.add(KEY_KEYSTORE_FILE + \" must be named with a .jceks, .p12, .pfx or .jks suffix\");\n   451\t            return false;\n   452\t        }\n   453\t        // Two independent copies of the password: the load and the entry read each get a buffer they\n   454\t        // own, so wiping one can never hand the other a zeroed key.\n   455\t        char[] loadPassword = password.clone();\n   456\t        char[] entryPassword = password.clone();\n   457\t        try {\n   458\t            KeyStore ks = KeyStore.getInstance(type);\n   459\t            try (java.io.InputStream in = Files.newInputStream(keystore)) {\n   460\t                ks.load(in, loadPassword);\n   461\t            }\n   462\t            if (!ks.containsAlias(alias)) {\n   463\t                problems.add(KEY_KEYSTORE_FILE + \" has no entry named by \" + KEY_PASSWORD_ENTRY_ALIAS);\n   464\t                return false;\n   465\t            }\n   466\t            KeyStore.ProtectionParameter protection = new KeyStore.PasswordProtection(entryPassword);\n   467\t            return entryIsUsable(ks, alias, protection, problems);\n   468\t        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n   469\t            // GeneralSecurityException covers a wrong password; the JDK throws IOException for\n   470\t            // that case too. Only the category is reported, never the message, which can quote a\n   471\t            // path.\n   472\t            problems.add(KEY_KEYSTORE_FILE + \" could not be opened with \" + KEY_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD + \" / \" + KEY_PASSWORD_ENTRY_ALIAS);\n   473\t            return false;\n   474\t        } finally {\n   475\t            java.util.Arrays.fill(loadPassword, (char) 0);\n   476\t            java.util.Arrays.fill(entryPassword, (char) 0);\n   477\t        }\n   478\t    }\n   479\t\n   480\t    /**\n   481\t     * Checks that the entry holds at least {@link PasswordHasher#MIN_PEPPER_BYTES} of key material.\n   482\t     *\n   483\t     * <p>{@link KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry} is the only accepted type because it is the only secret\n   484\t     * entry type the JDK has ({@code PrivateKeyEntry} and {@code TrustedCertificateEntry} are the\n   485\t     * others). It is also the entry {@code keytool} actually produces for a symmetric secret -\n   486\t     * {@code keytool -importpass} on this JDK either fails outright or files the value under a\n   487\t     * private-key entry, which is refused here. Certificates and private keys are refused: neither is\n   488\t     * a MAC key.\n   489\t     */\n   490\t    private static boolean entryIsUsable(KeyStore ks, String alias, KeyStore.ProtectionParameter protection,\n   491\t            List<String> problems) throws GeneralSecurityException, IOException {\n   492\t        KeyStore.Entry entry = ks.getEntry(alias, protection);\n   493\t        if (!(entry instanceof KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry)) {\n   494\t            problems.add(KEY_KEYSTORE_FILE + \" entry named by \" + KEY_PASSWORD_ENTRY_ALIAS\n   495\t                    + \" is not a symmetric secret key entry\");\n   496\t            return false;\n   497\t        }\n   498\t        byte[] encoded = ((KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry) entry).getSecretKey().getEncoded();\n   499\t        boolean longEnough = encoded != null && encoded.length >= PasswordHasher.MIN_PEPPER_BYTES;\n   500\t        if (encoded != null) {\n   501\t            java.util.Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n   502\t        }\n   503\t        if (!longEnough) {\n   504\t            problems.add(KEY_KEYSTORE_FILE + \" secret key entry is shorter than \"\n   505\t                    + PasswordHasher.MIN_PEPPER_BYTES + \" bytes\");\n   506\t            return false;\n   507\t        }\n   508\t        return true;\n   509\t    }\n   510\t\n   511\t That includes not\n    17\t * shipping even a package-private helper that hashes a supplied password into a stored record: such a\n    18\t * helper is account creation under another name, and a reviewer would be right to report it as\n    19\t * scope creep the moment it exists in production code. Tests build their own credential files in\n    20\t * {@code FixtureProvisioner}, against the same {@link PasswordHasher} this service verifies with, so\n    21\t * a fixture record still exercises the loader instead of restating it.\n    22\t *\n    23\t * <h2>Order of operations for one attempt</h2>\n    24\t * <ol>\n    25\t *   <li><b>Source address.</b> The caller-supplied address is parsed textually into canonical form\n    26\t *       by {@link InputValidator#normalizeSourceAddress}. A missing or unparseable address ends the\n    27\t *       attempt immediately: without it the attempt cannot be rate limited, and the throttle is part\n    28\t *       of the authentication decision. The answer is a fixed \"authentication is not available for\n    29\t *       this request\" that names no account, so it does not leak existence.</li>\n    30\t *   <li><b>Per-source budget.</b> Consumed before any credential work, so an attacker cannot buy a\n    31\t *       full PBKDF2 round per attempt beyond their budget.</li>\n    32\t *   <li><b>Per-account lock.</b> Applied read-only: probing a locked account cannot lengthen the\n    33\t *       lock, and the account's budget is never charged by another account's traffic.</li>\n    34\t *   <li><b>Validation</b> of the username and password shapes. Rejections are denial-shaped.</li>\n    35\t *   <li><b>One credential derivation</b>, for a known name and for an unknown name alike\n    36\t *       ({@link CredentialVerifier}).</li>\n    37\t *   <li><b>Failure bookkeeping</b>, then <b>session issue</b>, under the state lock, so the\n    38\t *       check-then-act on the failure counters and on session capacity is atomic.</li>\n    39\t *   <li><b>The event is persisted.</b> If the log write fails, the attempt is refused - including a\n    40\t *       otherwise-successful one. Authentication does not proceed unrecorded.</li>\n    41\t *   <li><b>Latency floor</b> ({@link LatencyBudget}) makes every outcome above take comparable\n    42\t *       time.</li>\n    43\t *   <li><b>Credential clearing.</b> The password copy held by this component is wiped in a\n    44\t *       {@code finally} block, whatever happened.</li>\n    45\t * </ol>\n    46\t *\n    47\t * <p><b>What a caller may learn.</b> Exactly three things: the generic denial text, the same generic\n    48\t * denial text with a retry hint, or success plus a session identifier. Unknown username, wrong\n    49\t * password, malformed username, unhashable password, locked account and throttled source all produce\n    50\t * byte-identical denial text; the last two additionally carry a retry hint, which describes the\n    51\t * caller's own request rate and not any account.\n    52\t *\n    53\t * <p><b>Thread safety.</b> Instances are safe for concurrent use. Credential state is read-only after\n    54\t * construction; throttle and session state are internally synchronised; the log channel is guarded by\n    55\t * its own monitor.\n    56\t */\n    57\tpublic final class AuthenticationService implements AutoCloseable {\n    58\t\n    59\t    /** Upper bound on concurrent credential derivations;
