This component works on a private copy and\n   102\t         *     wipes that copy; the caller must wipe its own array as soon as\n   103\t         *     {@link AuthenticationService#authenticate(Attempt)} returns.\n   104\t         * @param sourceAddress the peer address as a bare IPv4/IPv6 literal, as resolved by the\n   105\t         *     transport. Never take this from {@code X-Forwarded-For} unless a trusted proxy set it.\n   106\t         */\n   107\t        public static Attempt of(String username, char[] submittedPassword, String sourceAddress) {\n   108\t            return new Attempt(username, submittedPassword, sourceAddress);\n   109\t        }\n   110\t\n   111\t        String username() {\n   112\t            return username;\n   113\t        }\n   114\t\n   115\t        char[] password() {\n   116\t            return password;\n   117\t        }\n   118\t\n   119\t        String sourceAddress() {\n   120\t            return sourceAddress;\n   121\t        }\n   122\t\n   123\t        @Override\n   124\t        public String toString() {\n   125\t            // Never render the password, even masked: an Attempt in a log line is already a defect.\n   126\t            return \"Attempt[source=\" + (sourceAddress == null ? \"unset\" : \"set\") + \"]\";\n   127\t        }\n   128\t    }\n   129\t\n   130\t    /**\n   131\t     * Authenticates one attempt. Never throws for a reason the caller can act on: every failure is a\n   132\t     * denial.\n   133\t     *\n   134\t     * @return the result to present to the user\n   135\t     */\n   136\t    public AuthResult authenticate(Attempt attempt) {\n   137\t        long startedNanos = System.nanoTime();\n   138\t        char[] passwordCopy = null;\n   139\t        byte[] passwordUtf8 = null;\n   140\t        String submitted = attempt == null ? null : attempt.username();\n   141\t        String canonicalSource = null;\n   142\t        String canonicalName = null;\n   143\t        boolean present = false;\n   144\t        String issuedSessionId = null;\n   145\t        boolean granted = false;\n   146\t        try {\n   147\t            if (attempt == null || attempt.password() == null) {\n   148\t                canonicalSource = InputValidator.normalizeSourceAddress(attempt == null ? null : attempt.sourceAddress());\n   149\t                record(EventType.LOGIN_REJECTED_NO_SOURCE, \"failure\", submitted, false, canonicalSource,\n   150\t                        Diagnostic.SOURCE_ADDRESS_MISSING, startedNanos, 0, null);\n   151\t                return AuthResult.sourceUnavailable();\n   152\t            }\n   153\t            canonicalSource = InputValidator.normalizeSourceAddress(attempt.sourceAddress());\n   154\t            if (canonicalSource == null) {\n   155\t                record(EventType.LOGIN_REJECTED_NO_SOURCE, \"failure\", submitted, false, null,\n   156\t                        Diagnostic.SOURCE_ADDRESS_MISSING, startedNanos, 0, null);\n   157\t                return AuthResult.sourceUnavailable();\n   158\t            }\n   159\t\n   160\t            long now = time.nowMillis();\n   161\t\n   162\t            // 1. Per-source budget, before any credential work.\n   163\t            AttemptThrottle.Decision sourceDecision = throttle.consumeSourceAttempt(canonicalSource, now,\n   164\t                    config.sourceWindowSeconds(), config.sourceMaxAttempts(), config.sourceBlockSeconds());\n   165\t            if (!sourceDecision.allowed()) {\n   166\t                record(EventType.LOGIN_THROTTLED, \"blocked\", submitted, false, canonicalSource,\n   167\t                        Diagnostic.CREDENTIAL_MISMATCH, startedNanos, sourceDecision.retryAfterSeconds(), null);\n   168\t                return AuthResult.rateLimited(sourceDecision.retryAfterSeconds());\n   169\t            }\n   170\t\n   171\t            // 2. Per-account lock, keyed on the submitted name so flood traffic against one name is\n   172\t            //    accounted to that name rather than to whichever spelling happens to exist.\n   173\t            String lockKey = AttemptThrottle.canonicalize(submitted);\n   174\t            AttemptThrottle.Decision lockDecision = throttle.checkAccountLock(lockKey, now);\n   175\t            if (!lockDecision.allowed()) {\n   176\t                record(EventType.LOGIN_THROTTLED, \"blocked\", submitted, false, canonicalSource,\n   177\t                        Diagnostic.CREDENTIAL_MISMATCH, startedNanos, lockDecision.retryAfterSeconds(), null);\n   178\t                return AuthResult.rateLimited(lockDecision.retryAfterSeconds());\n   179\t            }\n   180\t\n   181\t            // 3. Input shapes.\n   182\t            String username = InputValidator.validateUsername(submitted);\n   183\t            if (username == null) {\n   184\t                // Burn a real derivation before refusing: \"that name is malformed\" must not come\n   185\t                // back measurably faster than \"that name does not exist\" or \"wrong password\", or the\n   186\t                // shape check becomes an account probe.\n   187\t                Diagnostic burn = burnUniformWork();\n   188\t                record(EventType.LOGIN_FAILURE, \"failure\", submitted, false, canonicalSource,\n   189\t                        burn, startedNanos, 0, null);\n   190\t                return AuthResult.denied();\n   191\t            }\n   192\t            passwordCopy = InputValidator.validatePassword(attempt.password());\n   193\t            passwordUtf8 = InputValidator.encodePassword(passwordCopy);\n   194\t            Arrays.fill(passwordCopy, (char) 0);\n   195\t            passwordCopy = null;\n   196\t\n   197\t            // 4. One derivation, whichever branch follows.\n   198\t            Optional<AccountCredentials> found = store.lookup(username);\n   199\t            canonicalName = AttemptThrottle.canonicalize(username);\n   200\t            present = found.isPresent();\n   201\t            AccountCredentials record = found.orElse(null);\n   202\t            boolean matched;\n   203\t            try {\n   204\t                matched = verifier.verify(passwordUtf8, record);\n   205\t            } catch (AuthException e) {\n   206\t                record(EventType.LOGIN_FAILURE, \"error\", submitted, present, canonicalSource,\n   207\t                        e.diagnostic(), startedNanos, 0, null);\n   208\t                return AuthResult.denied();\n   209\t            }\n   210\t\n   211\t            if (!matched) {\n   212\t                AttemptThrottle.Decision afterFailure = throttle.recordAccountFailure(canonicalName,\n   213\t                        time.nowMillis(), config.accountLockAfter(), config.accountLockSeconds());\n   214\t                record(EventType.LOGIN_FAILURE, \"failure\", submitted, present, canonicalSource,\n   215\t                        Diagnostic.CREDENTIAL_MISMATCH, startedNanos, 0, null);\n   216\t                // The retry hint from a lock is withheld here: the denial for a wrong password and\n   217\t                // the denial that trips the lock must be indistinguishable, so the first refusal\n   218\t                // after locking carries no hint either.\n   219\t                if (afterFailure.kind() == AttemptThrottle.Kind.LOCKED) {\n   220\t                    logLockEngaged(submitted, present, canonicalSource,\n   221\t                            afterFailure.retryAfterSeconds(), startedNanos);\n   222\t                }\n   223\t                return AuthResult.denied();\n   224\t            }\n   225\t\n   226\t            throttle.resetAccount(canonicalName);\n   227\t            String sessionId = sessions.issue();\n   228\t            issuedSessionId = sessionId;\n   229\t            if (sessionId == null) {\n   230\t                record(EventType.LOGIN_FAILURE, \"error\", submitted, true, canonicalSource,\n   231\t                        Diagnostic.SESSION_STORE_LIMIT, startedNanos, 0, null);\n   232\t                return AuthResult.denied();\n   233\t            }\n   234\t            record(EventType.LOGIN_SUCCESS, \"success\", submitted, true, canonicalSource, null,\n   235\t                    startedNanos, 0, sessionId);\n   236\t            granted = true;\n   237\t            return AuthResult.success(sessionId);\n   238\t        } catch (AuthException e) {\n   239\t            if (e.isUniformDenial()) {\n   240\t                try {\n   241\t                    record(EventType.LOGIN_FAILURE, \"failure\", submitted, false, canonicalSource,\n   242\t                            e.diagnostic(), startedNanos, 0, null);\n   243\t                } catch (AuditPersistenceException ignored) {\n   244\t                    alertAround(ignored);\n   245\t                }\n   246\t                return AuthResult.denied();\n   247\t            }\n   248\t            try {\n   249\t                record(EventType.SECURITY_ALERT, \"error\", submitted, present, canonicalSource,\n   250\t                        e.diagnostic(), startedNanos, 0, null);\n   251\t            } catch (AuditPersistenceException ignored) {\n   252\t                alertAround(ignored);\n   253\t            }\n   254\t            return AuthResult.denied();\n   255\t        } catch (AuditPersistenceException e) {\n   256\t            // Fail closed: an attempt whose event cannot be persisted is not granted, and an\n   257\t            // otherwise successful one loses its session.\n   258\t            //\n   259\t            // Nothing is charged to the account here. A wrong password has already been charged -\n   260\t            // the mismatch path calls recordAccountFailure before it records, so charging again here\n   261\t            // would double-count it. What this catch is reachable *for* is the success path, where a\n   262\t            // charge would punish a correct password for the log being broken: a full file system or\n   263\t            // a rotated-out log would then convert every user's own successful sign-in into a\n   264\t            // consecutive failure, and after accountLockAfter of them the account locks itself out of\n   265\t            // an authentication service whose only fault was storage. That is the rule-3 failure this\n   266\t            // component is specifically required not to have - a limit that locks out a legitimate\n   267\t            // user for reasons that are not their credentials.\n   268\t            //\n   269\t            // Refusing still costs the attacker something: the per-source budget is consumed before\n   270\t            // any credential work on every attempt, so an outage is not a window of free guessing.\n   271\t            alertAround(e);\n   272\t            return AuthResult.denied();\n   273\t        } catch (RuntimeException e) {\n   274\t            try {\n   275\t                record(EventType.SECURITY_ALERT, \"error\", submitted, present, canonicalSource,\n   276\t                        Diagnostic.INTERNAL, startedNanos, 0, null);\n   277\t            } catch (AuditPersistenceException | RuntimeException ignored) {\n   278\t                audit.recordAlert(Diagnostic.INTERNAL, submitted, canonicalSource);\n   279\t            }\n   280\t            return AuthResult.denied();\n   281\t        } finally {\n   282\t            if (passwordCopy != null) {\n   283\t                Arrays.fill(passwordCopy, (char) 0);\n   284\t            }\n   285\t            if (passwordUtf8 != null) {\n   286\t                Arrays.fill(passwordUtf8, (byte) 0);\n   287\t            }\n   288\t            if (issuedSessionId != null && !granted) {\n   289\t                // An identifier can exist here without the caller ever receiving it: the success\n   290\t                // event may have failed to persist, or a later step may have thrown. Leaving it in\n   291\t                // the table would hand out a credential that authenticates as that account, is\n   292\t                // never counted anywhere as issued, and cannot be revoked by anything that follows,\n   293\t                // because nothing knows it was ever supposed to be dead.\n   294\t                sessions.revoke(issuedSessionId);\n   295\t            }\n   296\t            budget.padUntil(startedNanos);\n   297\t        }\n   298\t    }\n   299\t\n   300\t    /**\n   301\t     * Whether a session identifier is currently valid. Validity is server-side state only; the\n   302\t     * caller must not treat a syntactically well-formed identifier as authenticated.\n   303\t     */\n   304\t    public boolean isSessionValid(String sessionId) {\n   305\t        return sessions.isValid(sessionId);\n   306\t    }\n   307\t\n   308\t    /** Revokes a session identifier, for the caller's sign-out. */\n   309\t    public void revokeSession(String sessionId) {\n   310\t        sessions.revoke(sessionId);\n   311\t    }\n   312\t\n   313\t    /**\n   314\t     * Builds the {@code Set-Cookie} directive for an issued identifier, with {@code HttpOnly},\n   315\t     * {@code Secure}, {@code SameSite=Strict} and the validated path. Returns {@code null} when the\n   316\t     * request is not known to be over TLS or an input is unusable, rather than emitting a cookie that\n   317\t     * could cross a trust boundary.\n   318\t     */\n   319\t    public String sessionCookieFor(AuthResult result, String cookieName, String path, boolean secureTransport) {\n   320\t        if (result == null || !result.isSuccess()) {\n   321\t            return null;\n   322\t        }\n   323\t        return sessions.cookieDirectiveFor(result.sessionId(), cookieName, path, secureTransport);\n   324\t    }\n   325\t\n   326\t    /** Seconds an identifier stays valid, for the caller's cookie {@code Max-Age}. */\n   327\t    public int sessionTtlSeconds() {\n   328\t        return sessions.ttlSeconds();\n   329\t    }\n   330\t\n   331\t    /**\n   332\t     * Live identifiers in the table. Internal: the start-up check and the tests use it to reconcile\n   333\t     * issued against revoked, which is how an identifier that was created but never handed to a\n   334\t     * caller - a credential nobody can sign out - becomes visible.\n   335\t     */\n   336\t    int activeSessionCount() {\n   337\t        return sessions.activeCount();\n   338\t    }\n   339\t\n   340\t    /**\n   341\t     * Events retained in memory, oldest first, bounded by {@code AUTH_MAX_EVENTS}. Review of the full\n   342\t     * history is done on the log file, which is the durable record.\n   343\t     */\n   344\t    public List<String> recentEvents() {\n   345\t        return audit.retained();\n   346\t    }\n   347\t\n   348\t    /** Events this process has persisted. */\n   349\t    public long persistedEventCount() {\n   350\t        return audit.totalRecorded();\n   351\t    }\n   352\t\n   353\t    /**\n   354\t     * Re-checks the conditions that must hold for authentication to be trusted right now: the log\n   355\t     * file is still openable with private permissions, the credential file still parses, and the\n   356\t     * clock is sane. Returns problem descriptions, empty when everything holds.\n   357\t     */\n   358\t    public List<String> selfCheck() {\n   359\t        List<String> problems = new ArrayList<>();\n   360\t        if (audit.isClosed()) {\n   361\t            problems.add(\"event log is closed\");\n   362\t        }\n   363\t        long unsaved = audit.unsavedEvents();\n   364\t        if (unsaved > 0) {\n   365\t            // Surfaced rather than swallowed: the refusal path is silent by design, so this is the\n   366\t            // only way an operator learns the log has a hole in it.\n   367\t            problems.add(unsaved + \" authentication events could not be persisted\");\n   368\t        }\n   369\t        if (store.size() == 0) {\n   370\t            problems.add(\"credential store has no records\");\n   371\t        }\n   372\t        long now = time.nowMillis();\n   373\t        if (now < 1_600_000_000_000L) {\n   374\t            problems.add(\"the service clock is before 2020 and lockout windows are unreliable\");\n   375\t        }\n   376\t        if (sessions.issuedCount() < sessions.evictedCount()) {\n   377\t            // Bookkeeping cannot go backwards on its own: an issue that was counted as an eviction\n   378\t            // without producing an identifier is a hole through which an identifier could exist that\n   379\t            // was never handed out.\n   380\t            problems.add(\"session accounting is inconsistent\");\n   381\t        }\n   382\t        return problems;\n   383\t    }\n   384\t\n   385\t    @Override\n   386\t    public void close() {\n   387\t        audit.close();\n   388\t        sessions.clear();\n   389\t        hasher.erase();\n   390\t    }\n   391\t\n   392\t    /**\n   393\t     * Runs one full derivation against the unknown-username parameters and reports the reason it\n   394\t     * ended with. Used so that a refusal which happens before any store lookup still costs a full\n   395\t     * derivation, in CPU as well as in wall clock.\n   396\t     */\n   397\t    private Diagnostic burnUniformWork() {\n   398\t        byte[] probe = \"uniform-work-probe\".getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n   399\t        try {\n   400\t            verifier.verify(probe, null);\n   401\t            return Diagnostic.USERNAME_INVALID;\n   402\t        } catch (AuthException e) {\n   403\t            return e.diagnostic();\n   404\t        } finally {\n   405\t            Arrays.fill(probe, (byte) 0);\n   406\t        }\n   407\t    }\n   408\t\n   409\t    /**\n   410\t     * Records that this attempt is the one that engaged the account lock.\n   411\t     *\n   412\t     * <p>{@code present} is the caller's, not a constant. This line sits immediately after the\n   413\t     * mismatch line for the same attempt, which already reported the flag; a second line for the same\n   414\t     * attempt that reports the opposite is not a missing detail, it is a contradiction in the record,\n   415\t     * and an operator reconciling the two would conclude the lock fired on a name with no record -\n   416\t     * i.e. that the throttle locked out somebody who does not exist.\n   417\t     */\n   418\t    private void logLockEngaged(String submitted, boolean present, String canonicalSource,\n   419\t            int retryAfterSeconds, long startedNanos) {\n   420\t        try {\n   421\t            record(EventType.LOGIN_THROTTLED, \"blocked\", submitted, present, canonicalSource,\n   422\t                    Diagnostic.CREDENTIAL_MISMATCH, startedNanos, retryAfterSeconds, null);\n   423\t        } catch (AuditPersistenceException ignored) {\n   424\t            alertAround(ignored);\n   425\t        }\n   426\t    }\n   427\t\n   428\t    /**\n   429\t     * Writes the alert that replaces an event which could not be persisted, keeping the account\n   430\t     * attribution that event had already computed. Returning {@code false} means the alert failed too\n   431\t     * and the caller must keep refusing.\n   432\t     */\n   433\t    private boolean alertAround(AuditPersistenceException failure) {\n   434\t        AuthEvent unsaved = failure.unsavedEvent();\n   435\t        if (unsaved != null) {\n   436\t            return audit.recordAlert(failure.diagnostic(), unsaved);\n   437\t        }\n   438\t        return audit.recordAlert(failure.diagnostic(), null, null);\n   439\t    }\n   440\t\n   441\t    private void record(EventType type, String outcome, String submitted, boolean present, String canonicalSource,\n   442\t            Diagnostic reason, long startedNanos, int retryAfterSeconds, String sessionId)\n   443\t            throws AuditPersistenceException {\n   444\t        AuthEvent event = AuthEvent.builder()\n   445\t                .timestampMillis(time.now().toEpochMilli())\n   446\t                .type(type)\n   447\t                .outcome(outcome)\n   448\t                .account(submitted, present)\n   449\t                .source(canonicalSource)\n   450\t                .reason(reason)\n   451\t                .latencyMicros(Math.max(0L, (System.nanoTime() - startedNanos) / 1000L))\n   452\t                .retryAfterSeconds(retryAfterSeconds)\n   453\t                .sessionSuffix(sessionId)\n   454\t                .build();\n   455\t        audit.record(event);\n   456\t    }\n   457\t\n   458\t    /** Fluent construction. {@link #build()} performs every start-up check and fails closed. */\n   459\t    public static Builder builder() {\n   460\t        return new Builder();\n   461\t    }\n   462\t\n   463\t    /** Assembles the service from configuration and a time source. */\n   464\t    public static final class Builder {\n   465\t\n   466\t        private AuthConfig config;\n   467\t        private TimeSource time;\n   468\t        private SecureRandom random;\n   469\t\n   470\t        private Builder() {\n   471\t        }\n   472\t\n   473\t        public Builder config(AuthConfig value) {\n   474\t            this.config = value;\n   475\t            return this;\n   476\t        }\n   477\t\n   478\t        public Builder timeSource(TimeSource value) {\n   479\t            this.time = value;\n   480\t            return this;\n   481\t        }\n   482\t\n   483\t        /**\n   484\t         * Optional, for tests. The service is not\n   499\t         *     constructed at all: there is no partially working instance, no permissive default and no\n   500\t         *     in-memory fallback credential.\n   501\t         */\n   502\t        public AuthenticationService build() throws InvalidConfigException {\n   503\t            List<String> problems = new ArrayList<>();\n   504\t            if (config == null) {\n   505\t                problems.add(\"configuration is required\");\n   506\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   507\t            }\n   508\t            TimeSource clock = time == null ? TimeSource.systemUtc() : time;\n   509\t            config.validateFilesystem();\n   510\t\n   511\t            List<String> storeProblems = new ArrayList<>();\n   512\t            FileCredentialStore loadedStore = FileCredentialStore.load(config.accountsFile(), config, storeProblems);\n   513\t            if (!storeProblems.isEmpty()) {\n   514\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(storeProblems);\n   515\t            }\n   516\t            if (loadedStore.size() == 0) {\n   517\t                problems.add(\"credential store contains no usable records\");\n   518\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   519\t            }\n   520\t\n   521\t            AuditLogger logger = AuditLogger.open(config.eventLogFile(), config.maxEvents(),\n   522\t                    config.minFreeBytes(), clock.nowMillis());\n   523\t\n   524\t            SecureRandom generator;\n   525\t            if (random != null) {\n   526\t                generator = random;\n   527\t            } else {\n   528\t                // The platform default rather than SecureRandom.getInstanceStrong(): on Linux the\n   529\t                // strong instance is NativePRNGBlocking, which reads /dev/random and can block\n   530\t                // indefinitely at start-up, turning an availability problem into a start-up failure.\n   531\t                // The default instance is the JCE cryptographically strong generator (NativePRNG,\n   532\t                // seeded from /dev/urandom) and is what the platform recommends for this use.\n   533\t                generator = new SecureRandom();\n   534\t            }\n   535\t\n   536\t            char[] pepper;\n   537\t            try {\n   538\t                pepper = KeystorePepperProvider.loadPepper(config.keystoreFile(),\n   539\t                        config.keystorePassword(), config.passwordEntryAlias());\n   540\t            } catch (AuthException e) {\n   541\t                problems.add(\"the credential hash key could not be loaded from the configured keystore\");\n   542\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   543\t            }\n   544\t\n   545\t            PasswordHasher passwordHasher;\n   546\t            CredentialVerifier credentialVerifier;\n   547\t            LatencyBudget latencyBudget;\n   548\t            try {\n   549\t                passwordHasher = new PasswordHasher(pepper, config.pbkdf2Iterations(), generator);\n   550\t            } catch (AuthException e) {\n   551\t                problems.add(\"the credential hash key is unusable\");\n   552\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   553\t            } finally {\n   554\t                Arrays.fill(pepper, (char) 0);\n   555\t            }\n   556\t\n   557\t            CredentialVerifier.AttemptParameters decoy;\n   558\t            try {\n   559\t                byte[] decoySalt = passwordHasher.newSalt();\n   560\t                byte[] decoyDigest = passwordHasher.derive(\"decoy-placeholder\".getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8),\n   561\t                        decoySalt, config.pbkdf2Iterations());\n   562\t                decoy = new CredentialVerifier.AttemptParameters(decoySalt, config.pbkdf2Iterations(), decoyDigest);\n   563\t            } catch (AuthException e) {\n   564\t                problems.add(\"the unknown-username work parameters could not be prepared\");\n   565\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   566\t            }\n   567\t\n   568\t            latencyBudget = LatencyBudget.measure(passwordHasher, config.pbkdf2Iterations());\n   569\t            credentialVerifier = new CredentialVerifier(passwordHasher, latencyBudget,\n   570\t                    MAX_CONCURRENT_DERIVATIONS, decoy);\n   571\t\n   572\t            config.eraseSecrets();\n   573\t\n   574\t            SessionTokenStore tokenStore = new SessionTokenStore(generator, config.sessionTtlSeconds(),\n   575\t                    config.maxActiveSessions(), clock);\n   576\t            AttemptThrottle attemptThrottle = new AttemptThrottle();\n   577\t            return new AuthenticationService(config, loadedStore, passwordHasher, credentialVerifier,\n   578\t                    attemptThrottle, tokenStore, logger, clock, latencyBudget);\n   579\t        }\n   580\t    }\n   581\t}\nFILE ConfigSource.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Collections;\n     4\timport java.util.LinkedHashMap;\n     5\timport java.util.LinkedHashSet;\n     6\timport java.util.Map;\n     7\timport java.util.Set;\n     8\t\n     9\t/**\n    10\t * Read-only view of operator-supplied configuration.\n    11\t *\n    12\t * <p>Every value obtained through this interface is treated as attacker-controlled: the process\n    13\t * environment can be influenced by anything that can write deployment files, and system properties\n    14\t * can be influenced by anything that can write the launch command line. Values are therefore never\n    15\t * used without the explicit validation performed in {@link AuthConfig}, and an unrecognised\n    16\t * {@code AUTH_}-prefixed key is a start-up error rather than something to ignore.\n    17\t */\n    18\tpublic interface ConfigSource {\n    19\t\n    20\t    /** Namespace whose unknown keys are rejected instead of ignored. */\n    21\t    String NAMESPACE = \"AUTH_\";\n    22\t\n    23\t    /** Returns the raw value for {@code key}, or {@code null} when absent. */\n    24\t    String get(String key);\n    25\t\n    26\t    /** Returns every key this source can see, including keys outside {@value #NAMESPACE}. */\n    27\t    Set<String> keys();\n    28\t\n    29\t    /** Configuration taken from the process environment. */\n    30\t    static ConfigSource ofEnvironment() {\n    31\t        final Map<String, String> snapshot = new LinkedHashMap<>(System.getenv());\n    32\t        return new SnapshotConfigSource(snapshot);\n    33\t    }\n    34\t\n    35\t    /** Configuration taken from an explicit map; used by tests and by embedded deployments. */\n    36\t    static ConfigSource ofMap(Map<String, String> values) {\n    37\t        return new SnapshotConfigSource(new LinkedHashMap<>(values));\n    38\t    }\n    39\t}\n    40\t\n    41\t/**\n    42\t * Immutable {@link ConfigSource}. The key set is snapshotted at construction so that\n    43\t * {@link AuthConfig} can detect unknown keys in the same namespace.\n    44\t */\n    45\tfinal class SnapshotConfigSource implements ConfigSource {\n    46\t\n    47\t    private final Map<String, String> values;\n    48\t    private final Set<String> keys;\n    49\t\n    50\t    SnapshotConfigSource(Map<String, String> values) {\n    51\t        this.values = Collections.unmodifiableMap(new LinkedHashMap<>(values));\n    52\t        this.keys = Collections.unmodifiableSet(new LinkedHashSet<>(values.keySet()));\n    53\t    }\n    54\t\n    55\t    @Override\n    56\t    public String get(String key) {\n    57\t        return values.get(key);\n    58\t    }\n    59\t\n    60\t    @Override\n    61\t    public Set<String> keys() {\n    62\t        return keys;\n    63\t    }\n    64\t\n    65\t    @Override\n    66\t    public String toString() {\n    67\t        // Never render values: they may contain paths or other operator-private material.\n    68\t        long inNamespace = keys.stream().filter(k -> k.startsWith(NAMESPACE)).count();\n    69\t        return \"SnapshotConfigSource[\" + keys.size() + \" keys, \" + inNamespace + \" in \" + NAMESPACE + \"]\";\n    70\t    }\n    71\t}\nFILE CredentialStore.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Optional;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * Read view of the server-side credential records.\n     7\t *\n     8\t * <p>Implementations resolve a submitted username to at most one record. The credential file is\n     9\t * required not to contain two records whose names differ only in ASCII case, so a lookup cannot\n    10\t * silently pick between two accounts; a store that detects such a collision refuses to start.\n    11\t */\n    12\tinterface CredentialStore {\n    13\t\n    14\t    /**\n    15\t     * Looks up a record.\n    16\t     *\n    17\t     * @param username a username that has already passed {@link InputValidator#validateUsername};\n    18\t     *     matching ignores ASCII case only, so a homoglyph or a Unicode look-alike never resolves to\n    19\t     *     an existing account\n    20\t     * @return the record, or {@link Optional#empty()} when no record matches\n    21\t     */\n    22\t    Optional<AccountCredentials> lookup(String username);\n    23\t\n    24\t    /** Number of records; used for start-up sanity checks only. */\n    25\t    int size();\n    26\t}\nFILE CredentialVerifier.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.concurrent.Semaphore;\n     4\timport java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit;\n     5\t\n     6\t/**\n     7\t * Checks one credential pair and shapes the work it does so that no failure outcome is\n     8\t * distinguishable from another by timing.\n     9\t *\n    10\t * <p><b>Comparable work for every outcome.</b> Each of these paths performs one full PBKDF2\n    11\t * derivation before it returns:\n    12\t * <ul>\n    13\t *   <li>username not in the store - the derivation uses a server-generated salt with the configured\n    14\t *       iteration count, and its result is discarded;</li>\n    15\t *   <li>username present, password wrong;</li>\n    16\t *   <li>username present, password correct.</li>\n    17\t * </ul>\n    18\t * After the derivation, every remaining difference is a field read or a map probe, and the latency\n    19\t * floor absorbs the residual spread.\n    20\t *\n    21\t * <p><b>Work is capped globally, not per key.</b> A fixed number of permits, handed out in FIFO\n    22\t * order, bounds how much hashing an attacker can force regardless of how many usernames or source\n    23\t * addresses the attempts are spread across. A caller that cannot obtain a permit within\n    24\t * {@value #PERMIT_TIMEOUT_MILLIS} ms is denied; attempts are never queued without bound, because an\n    25\t * unbounded queue is the denial of service this exists to prevent. Waiting for a permit also counts\n    26\t * against the latency floor, so a saturated service is uniformly slower - it cannot be made to run\n    27\t * one outcome fast and another slow.\n    28\t *\n    29\t * <p><b>No latch.</b> Saturating the permits denies only the attempts that arrive while saturated;\n    30\t * permits are released in a {@code finally} block and the next attempt proceeds normally.\n    31\t */\n    32\tfinal class CredentialVerifier {\n    33\t\n    34\t    /** Longest a caller will wait for a work permit before being denied. */\n    35\t    static final long PERMIT_TIMEOUT_MILLIS = 2_000L;\n    36\t\n    37\t    private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n    38\t    private final LatencyBudget budget;\n    39\t    private final Semaphore workPermits;\n    40\t    private final int maxConcurrentDerivations;\n    41\t    private final AttemptParameters decoy;\n    42\t\n    43\t    CredentialVerifier(PasswordHasher hasher, LatencyBudget budget, int maxConcurrentDerivations,\n    44\t            AttemptParameters decoy) {\n    45\t        if (maxConcurrentDerivations < 1) {\n    46\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"maxConcurrentDerivations\");\n    47\t        }\n    48\t        this.hasher = hasher;\n    49\t        this.budget = budget;\n    50\t        this.workPermits = new Semaphore(maxConcurrentDerivations, true);\n    51\t        this.maxConcurrentDerivations = maxConcurrentDerivations;\n    52\t        this.decoy = decoy;\n    53\t    }\n    54\t\n    55\t    /**\n    56\t     * Verifies a password against the record for a resolved username.\n    57\t     *\n    58\t     * @param passwordUtf8 validated UTF-8 bytes of the candidate password\n    59\t     * @param record the record for the resolved username, or {@code null} when there is none\n    60\t     * @return {@code true} only when the digest matches\n    61\t     * @throws AuthException when the check could not be performed at all - no permit, no decoy\n    62\t     *     parameters, or a store failure. The caller must deny the attempt and must not treat this\n    63\t     *     as either a match or a distinguishable kind of failure.\n    64\t     */\n    65\t    boolean verify(byte[] passwordUtf8, AccountCredentials record) throws AuthException {\n    66\t        if (passwordUtf8 == null || passwordUtf8.length == 0) {\n    67\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n    68\t        }\n    69\t        if (decoy == null) {\n    70\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    71\t        }\n    72\t        boolean acquired;\n    73\t        try {\n    74\t            acquired = workPermits.tryAcquire(PERMIT_TIMEOUT_MILLIS, TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS);\n    75\t        } catch (InterruptedException e) {\n    76\t            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();\n    77\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.WORK_UNAVAILABLE);\n    78\t        }\n    79\t        if (!acquired) {\n    80\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.WORK_UNAVAILABLE);\n    81\t        }\n    82\t        long started = System.nanoTime();\n    83\t        try {\n    84\t            final boolean matched;\n    85\t            if (record == null) {\n    86\t                // Discarded deliberately: the purpose is that an unknown username costs exactly what\n    87\t                // a known one costs.\n    88\t                hasher.matches(passwordUtf8, decoy.salt(), decoy.iterations(), decoy.digest());\n    89\t                matched = false;\n    90\t            } else {\n    91\t                matched = hasher.matches(passwordUtf8, record.salt(), record.iterations(), record.digest());\n    92\t            }\n    93\t            budget.padUntil(started);\n    94\t            return matched;\n    95\t        } finally {\n    96\t            workPermits.release();\n    97\t        }\n    98\t    }\n    99\t\n   100\t    /** Derivations the service will run at once. */\n   101\t    int maxConcurrentDerivations() {\n   102\t        return maxConcurrentDerivations;\n   103\t    }\n   104\t\n   105\t    /** Salt, iteration count and digest used to burn work for an unknown username. */\n   106\t    static final class AttemptParameters {\n   107\t\n   108\t        private final byte[] salt;\n   109\t        private final int iterations;\n   110\t        private final byte[] digest;\n   111\t\n   112\t        AttemptParameters(byte[] salt, int iterations, byte[] digest) {\n   113\t            this.salt = salt;\n   114\t            this.iterations = iterations;\n   115\t            this.digest = digest;\n   116\t        }\n   117\t\n   118\t        byte[] salt() {\n   119\t            return salt.clone();\n   120\t        }\n   121\t\n   122\t        int iterations() {\n   123\t            return iterations;\n   124\t        }\n   125\t\n   126\t        byte[] digest() {\n   127\t            return digest.clone();\n   128\t        }\n   129\t    }\n   130\t}\nFILE Diagnostic.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * Internal reason categories. Each carries a fixed, operator-facing explanation that is safe to\n     5\t * write to the log; none of them can contain a username, a password, an address or a token.\n     6\t */\n     7\tenum Diagnostic {\n     8\t\n     9\t    CREDENTIAL_MISMATCH(\"supplied password does not match the stored verifier\"),\n    10\t    SOURCE_ADDRESS_MISSING(\"no valid source address was supplied for the attempt\"),\n    11\t    USERNAME_INVALID(\"username failed validation before any store lookup\"),\n    12\t    SESSION_STORE_LIMIT(\"in-memory session capacity is exhausted\"),\n    13\t    WORK_UNAVAILABLE(\"the credential check could not be performed within the work budget\"),\n    14\t    PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED(\"password contains code points that cannot be encoded exactly\"),\n    15\t    PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT(\"password failed the length policy\"),\n    16\t    PASSWORD_CONTROL_CHARACTER(\"password contains a control character\"),\n    17\t    STORE_UNAVAILABLE(\"credential store could not be read\"),\n    18\t    LOG_WRITE_FAILED(\"event could not be persisted to the log file\"),\n    19\t    LOG_STORAGE_FULL(\"the event log file system is below the configured free-space floor\"),\n    20\t    LOG_FILE_LIMIT(\"the event log file reached the accepted size limit and must be rotated\"),\n    21\t    LOG_PARTIAL_RECORD(\"a partial record at the end of the event log was discarded\"),\n    22\t    LOG_LINE_REJECTED(\"a log line failed the safety checks before it could be written\"),\n    23\t    AUDIT_UNAVAILABLE(\"the audit logger is closed or was never built\"),\n    24\t    INTERNAL(\"unexpected internal failure\");\n    25\t\n    26\t    private final String safeDetail;\n    27\t\n    28\t    Diagnostic(String safeDetail) {\n    29\t        this.safeDetail = safeDetail;\n    30\t    }\n    31\t\n    32\t    /** Fixed operator-facing text; No URL, ZIP or remote URI scheme is accepted anywhere; the path\n    21\t * must be an absolute local path, checked for {@code NOFOLLOW_LINKS} regular-file status before it\n    22\t * is read.\n    23\t *\n    24\t * <p>Record format, one record per line, UTF-8:\n    25\t * <pre>username : base64(16-byte salt) : iteration-count : base64(32-byte digest)</pre>\n    26\t * A valid username cannot contain {@code :}, so the split is unambiguous. Rejected outright, each\n    27\t * with a start-up problem that names only the line number and never the offending content:\n    28\t * <ul>\n    29\t *   <li>a username that fails {@link InputValidator};</li>\n    30\t *   <li>Base64 that is not canonical, or of the wrong length;</li>\n    31\t *   <li>an iteration count below the configured minimum or above the hard maximum;</li>\n    32\t *   <li>duplicate usernames, and usernames that collide with another record ignoring ASCII case -\n    33\t *       either would let one spelling name two different records, so the store refuses to load;</li>\n    34\t *   <li>more records than {@link #MAX_RECORDS}, so the file cannot be used to exhaust memory.</li>\n    35\t * </ul>\n    36\t */\n    37\tfinal class FileCredentialStore implements CredentialStore {\n    38\t\n    39\t    static final int MAX_RECORDS = 20_000;\n    40\t    private static final int MAX_FILE_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024;\n    41\t    private static final int MAX_LINE_CHARS = 512;\n    42\t\n    43\t    private final Map<String, AccountCredentials> byExactName;\n    44\t    private final Map<String, String> canonicalByLowerName;\n    45\t\n    46\t    private FileCredentialStore(Map<String, AccountCredentials> byExactName, Map<String, String> canonicalByLowerName) {\n    47\t        this.byExactName = Collections.unmodifiableMap(byExactName);\n    48\t        this.canonicalByLowerName = Collections.unmodifiableMap(canonicalByLowerName);\n    49\t    }\n    50\t\n    51\t    /**\n    52\t     * Loads and validates the credential file.\n    53\t     *\n    54\t     * @param problems start-up problems are appended here; nothing is thrown so that the caller can\n    55\t     *     report every problem found across configuration and store together\n    56\t     */\n    57\t    static FileCredentialStore load(Path file, AuthConfig config, List<String> problems) {\n    58\t        Map<String, AccountCredentials> byExactName = new LinkedHashMap<>();\n    59\t        Map<String, String> canonicalByLowerName = new LinkedHashMap<>();\n    60\t        if (file == null) {\n    61\t            problems.add(\"credential file is missing\");\n    62\t            return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n    63\t        }\n    64\t        if (!Files.isRegularFile(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n    65\t            problems.add(\"credential file is not a regular file\");\n    66\t            return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n    67\t        }\n    68\t determined\");\n    73\t            return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n    74\t        }\n    75\t        if (size > MAX_FILE_BYTES) {\n    76\t            problems.add(\"credential file is larger than the accepted size\");\n    77\t            return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n    78\t        }\n    79\t        final     problems.add(\"credential file could not be read as UTF-8 text\");\n    84\t            return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n    85\t        }\n    86\t        if (lines.size() > MAX_RECORDS) {\n    87\t            problems.add(\"credential file has more records than the accepted maximum\");\n    88\t            return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n    89\t        }\n    90\t\n    91\t        int lineNumber = 0;\n    92\t        for (String line : lines) {\n    93\t            lineNumber++;\n    94\t            if (line.isEmpty() || line.startsWith(\"#\")) {\n    95\t                continue;\n    96\t            }\n    97\t            if (line.length() > MAX_LINE_CHARS) {\n    98\t                problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" is too long\");\n    99\t                continue;\n   100\t            }\n   101\t            AccountCredentials record = parseLine(line, lineNumber, config, problems);\n   102\t            if (record == null) {\n   103\t                continue;\n   104\t            }\n   105\t            String exact = record.username();\n   106\t lineNumber + \" collides with an earlier username ignoring case\");\n   113\t                continue;\n   114\t            }\n   115\t            byExactName.put(exact, record);\n   116\t            canonicalByLowerName.put(lower, exact);\n   117\t        }\n   118\t        if (!problems.isEmpty()) {\n   119\t            // Fail closed: a store we cannot fully trust is not loaded at all. The caller refuses\n   120\t            // to start the service once the collected problems are reported.\n   121\t            return new FileCredentialStore(new LinkedHashMap<>(), new LinkedHashMap<>());\n   122\t        }\n   123\t        return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n   124\t    }\n   125\t\n   126\t    private static AccountCredentials parseLine(String line, int lineNumber, AuthConfig config, List<String> problems) {\n   127\t        String[] parts = line.split(\":\", -1);\n   128\t        if (parts.length != 4) {\n   129\t            problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" does not have four fields\");\n   130\t            return null;\n   131\t        }\n   132\t        String username = InputValidator.validateUsername(parts[0]);\n   133\t        if (username == null) {\n   134\t            problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" has an invalid username\");\n   135\t            return null;\n   136\t        }\n   137\t        byte[] salt = PasswordHasher.decodeBytes(parts[1], PasswordHasher.SALT_BYTES);\n   138\t        if (salt == null) {\n   139\t            problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" has an invalid salt\");\n   140\t            return null;\n   141\t        }\n   142\t        int iterations = parseIterations(parts[2]);\n   143\t        // The upper bound is the configured count, not the parser's ceiling. A record may not order\n   144\t        // this deployment to do more work per sign-in than its own policy costs every other account:\n   145\t        // PasswordHasher enforces the same bound at derivation time, and refusing here turns it into\n   146\t        // a named start-up problem instead of a per-attempt store failure.\n   147\t        if (iterations < config.minAcceptedIterations() || iterations > config.pbkdf2Iterations()) {\n   148\t            problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" has an unacceptable iteration count\");\n   149\t            return null;\n   150\t        }\n   151\t        byte[] digest = PasswordHasher.decodeBytes(parts[3], PasswordHasher.MAX_DIGEST_BYTES);\n   152\t        if (digest == null) {\n   153\t            problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" has an invalid digest\");\n   154\t            return null;\n   155\t        }\n   156\t        return new AccountCredentials(username, salt, iterations, digest);\n   157\t    }\n   158\t\n   159\t    private static int parseIterations(String field) {\n   160\t        if (field.isEmpty() || field.length() > 9) {\n   161\t            return -1;\n   162\t        }\n   163\t        for (int i = 0; i < field.length(); i++) {\n   164\t            if (field.charAt(i) < '0' || field.charAt(i) > '9') {\n   165\t                return -1;\n   166\t            }\n   167\t        }\n   168\t        if (field.length() > 1 && field.charAt(0) == '0') {\n   169\t            return -1;\n   170\t        }\n   171\t        try {\n   172\t            return Integer.parseInt(field);\n   173\t        } catch (NumberFormatException e) {\n   174\t            return -1;\n   175\t        }\n   176\t    }\n   177\t\n   178\t    @Override\n   179\t    public Optional<AccountCredentials> lookup(String username) {\n   180\t        if (username == null) {\n   181\t            return Optional.empty();\n   182\t        }\n   183\t        String canonical = resolve(username);\n   184\t        if (canonical == null) {\n   185\t            return Optional.empty();\n   186\t        }\n   187\t        AccountCredentials record = byExactName.get(canonical);\n   188\t        return record == null ? ASCII case is the only thing\n   193\t     * folded, and load-time validation guarantees at most one record per folded name.\n   194\t     */\n   195\t    String resolve(String username) {\n   196\t        if (username == null) {\n   197\t            return null;\n   198\t        }\n   199\t        return canonicalByLowerName.get(lowerAscii(username));\n   200\t    }\n   201\t\n   202\t    @Override\n   203\t    public int size() {\n   204\t        return byExactName.size();\n   205\t    }\n   206\t\n   207\t    /**\n   208\t     * ASCII-only case folding. It is used solely to detect two records whose names differ only in\n   209\t     * case and to let one record be named by either spelling. The stored name is never rewritten, so\n   210\t     * this cannot merge two records and cannot make a non-ASCII look-alike resolve to an account.\n   211\t     */\n   212\t    private static String lowerAscii(String value) {\n   213\t        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(value.length());\n   214\t        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n   215\t            char c = value.charAt(i);\n   216\t            if (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') {\n   217\t                c = (char) (c + 32);\n   218\t            }\n   219\t            out.append(c);\n   220\t        }\n   221\t        return out.toString();\n   222\t    }\n   223\t}\nFILE InputValidator.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.net.Inet4Address;\n     4\timport java.net.Inet6Address;\n     5\timport java.net.InetAddress;\n     6\timport java.net.UnknownHostException;\n     7\timport java.nio.ByteBuffer;\n     8\timport java.nio.CharBuffer;\n     9\timport java.nio.charset.CharsetEncoder;\n    10\timport java.nio.charset.CoderResult;\n    11\timport java.nio.charset.CodingErrorAction;\n    12\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n    13\timport java.util.ArrayList;\n    14\timport java.util.Collections;\n    15\timport java.util.List;\n    16\t\n    17\t/**\n    18\t * Textual input validation for the two client-controlled strings this component accepts.\n    19\t *\n    20\t * <p>Both checks are fail-closed: anything not explicitly allowed is rejected, there is no\n    21\t * normalisation that could fold two different inputs onto the same account, and no rejection ever\n    22\t * echoes the offending value.\n    23\t */\n    24\tpublic final class InputValidator {\n    25\t\n    26\t    /** Longest username accepted. */\n    27\t    public static final int MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS = 64;\n    28\t\n    29\t    /**\n    30\t     * Shortest username accepted. The checks below reject every value the\n    64\t     * JDK would otherwise accept as a literal that is not a plain address, in particular:\n    65\t     * <ul>\n    66\t     *   <li>hostnames and anything else requiring resolution;</li>\n    67\t     *   <li>IPv6 zone identifiers ({@code fe80::1%eth0}) - the name after {@code %} is caller\n    68\t     *       chosen, so it would reintroduce hostnames and make buckets forgeable;</li>\n    69\t     *   <li>URL-looking values carrying a scheme, port, path or query;</li>\n    70\t     *   <li>bracketed forms ({@code [::1]}), surrounding whitespace, control characters, NUL.</li>\n    71\t     * </ul>\n    72\t     *\n    73\t     * <p>Remaining risk, documented in the README: raw literal addresses cannot distinguish\n    74\t     * clients behind one NAT gateway or behind a shared proxy, so the per-source budget is shared\n    75\t     * by everyone appearing to come from the same address. Deployments behind a proxy must pass the\n    76\t     * real client address; whatever that value is, it is still validated here before use.\n    77\t     */\n    78\t    public static String normalizeSourceAddress(String raw) {\n    79\t        String literalText = normalizeLiteralText(raw);\n    80\t        if (literalText == null) {\n    81\t            return null;\n    82\t        }\n    83\t        final InetAddress address;\n    84\t        try {\n    85\t            address = InetAddress.getByName(literalText);\n    86\t        } catch (UnknownHostException e) {\n    87\t            // getByName on a validated literal only fails for a malformed address.\n    88\t            return null;\n    89\t        }\n    90\t        if (!(address instanceof Inet4Address) && !(address instanceof Inet6Address)) {\n    91\t            return null;\n    92\t        }\n    93\t        return canonicalForm(address);\n    94\t    }\n    95\t\n    96\t    /**\n    97\t     * Purely textual check that {@code raw} is a bare IPv4 or IPv6 literal, and nothing else. No\n    98\t     * name resolution happens here.\n    99\t     */\n   100\t    private static String normalizeLiteralText(String raw) {\n   101\t        if (raw == null) {\n   102\t            return null;\n   103\t        }\n   104\t        if (raw.isEmpty() || raw.length() > MAX_SOURCE_ADDRESS_CHARS) {\n   105\t            return null;\n   106\t        }\n   107\t        for (int i = 0; i < raw.length(); i++) {\n   108\t            char c = raw.charAt(i);\n   109\t            // Control characters and spaces cannot appear in an address; '%', '/' and '@' are the\n   110\t            // zone-id, CIDR and userinfo forms that would either carry a caller-chosen name or\n   111\t            // describe a range rather than one host.\n   112\t            if (c <= 0x20 || c == 0x7f || c == '%' || c == '/' || c == '@' || c == '#') {\n   113\t                return null;\n   114\t            }\n   115\t        }\n   116\t        boolean v6 = raw.indexOf(':') >= 0;\n   117\t        if (v6) {\n   118\t            // A bare IPv6 literal contains only hex digits and colons, plus one embedded strict\n   119\t            // dotted quad as its tail (the IPv4-mapped form \"::ffff:203.0.113.7\" that dual-stack\n   120\t            // stacks and proxies emit for IPv4 peers). i < raw.length(); i++) {\n   142\t                    char c = raw.charAt(i);\n   143\t                    boolean allowed = (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'F') || c == ':';\n   144\t                    if (!allowed) {\n   145\t                        return null;\n   146\t                    }\n   147\t                }\n   148\t            }\n   149\t            if (countChar(raw, ':') < 2) {\n   150\t                return null;\n   151\t            }\n   152\t        } else {\n   153\t            for (int i = 0; Returns the value unchanged on success, {@code null} on rejection.\n   212\t     *\n   213\t     * <p>Allowed: Unicode letters and decimal digits as {@link Character#isLetterOrDigit(int)}\n   214\t     * defines them, plus {@code . _ - @}. Rejected: empty, shorter than\n   215\t     * {@value #MIN_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS} or longer than {@value #MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS} code points,\n   216\t     * a leading or trailing {@code .} or {@code -}, a trailing {@code _}, any {@code ..}, control\n   217\t     * characters, spaces, combining marks, emoji, and any code point outside the allowed set. A\n   218\t     * leading {@code _} is allowed: it is a common identifier prefix and, unlike {@code .} or\n   219\t     * {@code -}, is never a separator, so it cannot fold two different names onto one.\n   220\t     *\n   221\t     * <p>Deliberately absent: case folding and Unicode normalisation. They are what make\n   222\t     * {@code Admin} / {@code admin} and precomposed / decomposed names collide, so the comparison\n   223\t     * here is exact and byte-for-byte after validation.\n   224\t     *\n   225\t     * <p><b>What that does not do.</b> Because {@code isLetterOrDigit} is Unicode-wide, a name\n   226\t     * written in another script survives - a Cyrillic {@code U+0430} is not refused as a homoglyph\n   227\t     * of the Latin {@code U+0061}. No folding happens anywhere else either: the credential lookup,\n   228\t     * the throttle key and the logged account hash all compare ASCII case only, so a look-alike\n   229\t     * name is a <em>different</em> account with its own bucket and its own {@code account_id},\n   230\t     * never a way to reach or shadow someone else's. The exposure left over is a human one - two\n   231\t     * records in the operator's own file can look alike on screen - which is why names come from\n   232\t     * that file and not from self-service registration. Suite 1 pins both halves: that such a name\n   233\t     * is accepted, and that it never resolves to a Latin account.\n   234\t     */\n   235\t    public static String validateUsername(String raw) {\n   236\t        if (raw == null || raw.isEmpty()) {\n   237\t            return null;\n   238\t        }\n   239\t        if (raw.length() > MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS * 2) {\n   240\t            return null;\n   241\t        }\n   242\t        if (!isAllowedUsernameText(raw)) {\n   243\t            return null;\n   244\t        }\n   245\t        int codePoints = raw.codePointCount(0, raw.length());\n   246\t        if (codePoints < MIN_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS || codePoints > MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS) {\n   247\t            return null;\n   248\t        }\n   249\t        if (raw.indexOf(\"..\") >= 0) {\n   250\t            return null;\n   251\t        }\n   252\t        int first = raw.codePointAt(0);\n   253\t        if (!isLetterOrDigit(first) && first != '_') {\n   254\t            return null;\n   255\t        }\n   256\t        int last = raw.offsetByCodePoints(0, codePoints - 1);\n   257\t        if (!isLetterOrDigit(raw.codePointAt(last))) {\n   258\t            return null;\n   259\t        }\n   260\t        return raw;\n   261\t    }\n   262\t\n   263\t    /** Returns {@code true} when {@code candidate} is a username this validator accepts. */\n   264\t    public static boolean isValidUsername(String candidate) {\n   265\t        return validateUsername(candidate) != null;\n   266\t    }\n   267\t\n   268\t    /**\n   269\t     * Validates a password and returns a private copy of its characters. The caller's array is\n   270\t     * never retained, and the caller must wipe its own copy.\n   271\t     *\n   272\t     * <p>Rejects: {@code null}; any character below {@code U+0021} or exactly {@code U+007F}, checked\n   273\t     * before the length rules so a zero-filled buffer cannot qualify as a long-enough password;\n   274\t     * length outside {@value #MIN_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS}..{@value #MAX_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS} code points\n   275\t     * (so a trailing tab or NUL can never be silently stripped to reach a valid password, and no\n   276\t     * control character can inject a log record); unpaired surrogates; and any character that the\n   277\t     * required encoding cannot represent exactly.\n   278\t     *\n   279\t     * @throws AuthException with {@link Diagnostic#PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED} when the value\n   280\t     *     contains code points that cannot survive the UTF-8 encoding used for hashing. Accepting\n   281\t     *     them would make two different passwords hash identically.\n   282\t     */\n   283\t    public static char[] validatePassword(char[] raw) throws AuthException {\n   284\t        if (raw == null) {\n   285\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n   286\t        }\n   287\t        if (raw.length == 0) {\n   288\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n   289\t        }\n   290\t        // First, whatever the length: a single disallowed character anywhere makes the credential\n   291\t        // unusable, and scanning now is what stops an all-NUL buffer from being accepted on the\n   292\t        // strength of its size alone.\n   293\t        for (char c : raw) {\n   294\t            if (c < 0x21 || c == 0x7f) {\n   295\t                throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_CONTROL_CHARACTER);\n   296\t            }\n   297\t        }\n   298\t        if (raw.length < MIN_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS || raw.length > MAX_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS * 2) {\n   299\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n   300\t        }\n   301\t        int codePoints = 0;\n   302\t        for (int i = 0; i < raw.length;) {\n   303\t            char high = raw[i];\n   304\t            if (Character.isSurrogate(high)) {\n   305\t                if (!Character.isHighSurrogate(high)) {\n   306\t                    throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n   307\t                }\n   308\t                if (i + 1 >= raw.length || !Character.isLowSurrogate(raw[i + 1])) {\n   309\t                    throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n   310\t                }\n   311\t                i += 2;\n   312\t            } else {\n   313\t                i += 1;\n   314\t            }\n   315\t            codePoints++;\n   316\t        }\n   317\t        if (codePoints < MIN_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS || codePoints > MAX_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS) {\n   318\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n   319\t        }\n   320\t        byte[] encoded = encodePassword(raw);\n   321\t        char[] copy = new char[raw.length];\n   322\t        System.arraycopy(raw, 0, copy, 0, raw.length);\n   323\t        java.util.Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n   324\t        return copy;\n   325\t    }\n   326\t\n   327\t    /**\n   328\t     * Encodes a password as UTF-8 exactly as it will be hashed.\n   329\t     *\n   330\t     * <p><b>No copy of the credential is ever made immutable.</b> The obvious implementation -\n   331\t     * {@code new String(raw).getBytes(UTF_8)} and a round trip back to a String - leaves the\n   332\t     * password in two {@code String}s that cannot be wiped and linger until the collector moves\n   333\t     * them, which is exactly what rule 5 asks to be narrowed. This instead encodes straight out of\n   334\t     * the caller's {@code char[]} through a reporting {@link CharsetEncoder}, so a character the\n   335\t     * encoding cannot represent is <em>reported</em> rather than silently replaced, and every buffer\n   336\t     * that holds plaintext is a zeroed array.\n   337\t     *\n   338\t     * <p>The refusal matters for correctness, not only hygiene: {@link String#getBytes(java.nio.charset.Charset)}\n   339\t     * never throws, it substitutes {@code '?'}. Without the report, {@code \"caf\\u00e9\"} under a lossy\n   340\t     * encoding and a literal {@code \"caf?\"} produce the same digest and become interchangeable\n   341\t     * credentials. An unpaired surrogate is refused by the same mechanism.\n   342\t     *\n   343\t     * @throws AuthException when the value cannot be encoded byte-for-byte exactly\n   344\t     */\n   345\t    public static byte[] encodePassword(char[] raw) throws AuthException {\n   346\t        if (raw == null) {\n   347\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n   348\t        }\n   349\t        // Bounded so the sizing arithmetic below cannot overflow; far above any password this\n   350\t        // component accepts.\n   351\t        if (raw.length > MAX_ENCODABLE_CHARS) {\n   352\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n   353\t        }\n   354\t        // UTF-8 needs at most three bytes per UTF-16 code unit (a surrogate pair is two units for\n   355\t        // four bytes), so this buffer cannot overflow.\n   356\t        byte[] oversized = new byte[Math.max(1, raw.length * 3)];\n   357\t        byte[] encoded;\n   358\t        CharBuffer input = CharBuffer.wrap(raw);\n   359\t        ByteBuffer output = ByteBuffer.wrap(oversized);\n   360\t        try {\n   361\t            CharsetEncoder encoder = StandardCharsets.UTF_8.newEncoder()\n   362\t                    .onMalformedInput(CodingErrorAction.REPORT)\n   363\t                    .onUnmappableCharacter(CodingErrorAction.REPORT);\n   364\t            CoderResult result = encoder.encode(input, output, true);\n   365\t            if (!result.isUnderflow()) {\n   366\t                throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n   367\t            }\n   368\t            if (!encoder.flush(output).isUnderflow()) {\n   369\t                throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n   370\t            }\n   371\t            int written = output.position();\n   372\t            if (oversized.length == written) {\n   373\t                encoded = oversized;\n   374\t            } else {\n   375\t                encoded = new byte[written];\n   376\t                System.arraycopy(oversized, 0, encoded, 0, written);\n   377\t            }\n   378\t        } catch (RuntimeException e) {\n   379\t            java.util.Arrays.fill(oversized, (byte) 0);\n   380\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n   381\t        }\n   382\t        java.util.Arrays.fill(oversized, (byte) 0);\n   383\t        if (encoded.length == 0) {\n   384\t            java.util.Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n   385\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n   386\t        }\n   387\t        return encoded;\n   388\t    }\n   389\t\n   390\t    static List<String> immutableCopy(List<String> values) {\n   391\t        return Collections.unmodifiableList(new ArrayList<>(values));\n   392\t    }\n   393\t\n   394\t    private static boolean isAllowedUsernameText(String raw) {\n   395\t        for (int i = 0; i < raw.length(); i++) {\n   396\t            char c = raw.charAt(i);\n   397\t            if (c <= 0x20 || c == 0x7f) {\n   398\t                return false;\n   399\t            }\n   400\t            if (c == '.' || c == '_' || c == '-' || c == '@') {\n   401\t                continue;\n   402\t            }\n   403\t            if (!isLetterOrDigit(c)) {\n   404\t                return false;\n   405\t            }\n   406\t        }\n   407\t        return true;\n   408\t    }\n   409\t\n   410\t    /**\n   411\t     * Letter-or-digit test that excludes combining marks and other non-spacing characters, which\n   412\t     * render invisibly on top of a preceding character and are a classic look-alike account trick.\n   413\t     */\n   414\t    private static boolean isLetterOrDigit(int codePoint) {\n   415\t        if (codePoint < 0x80) {\n   416\t            return (codePoint >= 'a' && codePoint <= 'z')\n   417\t                    || (codePoint >= 'A' && codePoint <= 'Z')\n   418\t                    || (codePoint >= '0' && codePoint <= '9');\n   419\t        }\n   420\t        if (!Character.isLetterOrDigit(codePoint)) {\n   421\t            return false;\n   422\t        }\n   423\t        int type = Character.getType(codePoint);\n   424\t        return type != Character.NON_SPACING_MARK\n   425\t                && type != Character.COMBINING_SPACING_MARK\n   426\t                && type != Character.ENCLOSING_MARK\n   427\t                && type != Character.FORMAT\n   428\t                && type != Character.PRIVATE_USE\n   429\t                && type != Character.SURROGATE\n   430\t                && type != Character.UNASSIGNED;\n   431\t    }\n   432\t\n   433\t    private static boolean isStrictDottedQuad(String raw) {\n   434\t        int octet = 0;\n   435\t        int digits = 0;\n   436\t        int octetsSeen = 0;\n   437\t        // The virtual terminator below is what catches a leading zero in the *last* octet: with the\n   438\t        // check only at a real '.', \"203.0.113.07\" slips through, and the JDK folds it onto\n   439\t        // \"203.0.113.7\", hiding two spellings of one host behind one accepted literal.\n   440\t        for (int i = 0; i <= raw.length(); i++) {\n   441\t            char c = i < raw.length() ? raw.charAt(i) : '.';\n   442\t            if (c == '.') {\n   443\t                if (digits == 0 || digits > 3) {\n   444\t                    return false;\n   445\t                }\n   446\t                if (digits > 1 && raw.charAt(i - digits) == '0') {\n   447\t                    return false;\n   448\t                }\n   449\t                if (octet > 255) {\n   450\t                    return false;\n   451\t                }\n   452\t                octetsSeen++;\n   453\t                octet = 0;\n   454\t                digits = 0;\n   455\t            } else if (c >= '0' && c <= '9') {\n   456\t                octet = octet * 10 + (c - '0');\n   457\t                digits++;\n   458\t                if (digits > 3) {\n   459\t                    return false;\n   460\t                }\n   461\t            } else {\n   462\t                return false;\n   463\t            }\n   464\t        }\n   465\t        return octetsSeen == 4;\n   466\t    }\n   467\t\n   468\t    private static int countChar(String value, char target) {\n   469\t        int n = 0;\n   470\t        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n   471\t            if (value.charAt(i) == target) {\n   472\t                n++;\n   473\t            }\n   474\t        }\n   475\t        return n;\n   476\t    }\n   477\t}\nFILE InvalidConfigException.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.ArrayList;\n     4\timport java.util.Collections;\n     5\timport java.util.List;\n     6\t\n     7\t/**\n     8\t * Raised at start-up when configuration is missing, malformed, out of range, or points at a file\n     9\t * whose permissions would expose credential material to other users.\n    10\t *\n    11\t * <p>Start-up must fail rather than degrade: a permissive default for a limit, an unwritable log,\n    12\t * or a group-readable keystore are all conditions under which authentication cannot be trusted.\n    13\t *\n    14\t * <p>Problem strings name configuration keys and fixed reasons. They never contain a configured\n    15\t * value, so the keystore password cannot reach a log or a terminal through this path. The\n    16\t * suppressed cause is deliberate: this exception is constructed around keystore failures whose JDK\n    17\t * messages quote the keystore path.\n    18\t */\n    19\tpublic final class InvalidConfigException extends Exception {\n    20\t\n    21\t    private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n    22\t\n    23\t    private final List<String> problems;\n    24\t\n    25\t    InvalidConfigException(List<String> problems) {\n    26\t        super(describe(problems), null, false, false);\n    27\t        this.problems = Collections.unmodifiableList(new ArrayList<>(problems));\n    28\t    }\n    29\t\n    30\t    /** Every problem found, in the order detected. Safe to log; contains no configured values. */\n    31\t    public List<String> problems() {\n    32\t        return problems;\n    33\t    }\n    34\t\n    35\t    private static String describe(List<String> problems) {\n    36\t        if (problems.isEmpty()) {\n    37\t            return \"configuration is invalid\";\n    38\t        }\n    39\t        if (problems.size() == 1) {\n    40\t            return \"configuration is invalid: \" + problems.get(0);\n    41\t        }\n    42\t        return \"configuration is invalid (\" + problems.size() + \" problems): \" + String.join(\"; \", problems);\n    43\t    }\n    44\t\n    45\t    /**\n    46\t     * Renders a configuration key name defensively.
