Nothing here accepts a secret from a\n    17\t * client, and nothing here prints one: the loaded characters are copied straight into\n    18\t * {@link PasswordHasher}, and every failure path reports only a category.\n    19\t *\n    20\t * <p>Guards applied to the attacker-controllable inputs (keystore path, entry alias, both\n    21\t * passwords):\n    22\t * <ul>\n    23\t *   <li>the path must be an absolute path to an existing regular file, checked without following\n    24\t *       symlinks, and must not be readable or writable by group or other users;</li>\n    25\t *   <li>the file type is taken from the configured suffix and must be {@code .jceks} or\n    26\t *       {@code .p12}/{@code .pfx}; an arbitrary {@code file://}, jar or ZIP URL is never\n    27\t *       resolved;</li>\n    28\t *   <li>the alias must match {@code [A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,128}} and must exist in the store;</li>\n    29\t *   <li>the entry must be a {@link KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry} whose encoded key is at least\n    30\t *       {@link PasswordHasher#MIN_PEPPER_BYTES} bytes; a private-key or certificate entry is\n    31\t *       refused.</li>\n    32\t * </ul>\n    33\t *\n    34\t * <p>A wrong keystore password surfaces from the JDK as either a {@code GeneralSecurityException}\n    35\t * or an {@code IOException}, so both are handled; the JDK message can quote the keystore path, so\n    36\t * no exception message is propagated.\n    37\t */\n    38\tfinal class KeystorePepperProvider {\n    39\t\n    40\t    private static final int MAX_ALIAS_CHARS = 128;\n    41\t\n    42\t    private KeystorePepperProvider() {\n    43\t    }\n    44\t\n    45\t    /**\n    46\t     * Loads the pepper.\n    47\t     *\n    48\t     * @return the pepper characters, which the caller must wipe after handing them to\n    49\t     *     {@link PasswordHasher}\n    50\t     * @throws AuthException when the keystore or the entry cannot be used; the diagnostic carries no\n    51\t     *     detail beyond the fixed {@link Diagnostic#STORE_UNAVAILABLE} text\n    52\t     */\n    53\t    static char[] loadPepper(Path keystoreFile, char[] keystorePassword, String alias) throws AuthException {\n    54\t        if (keystoreFile == null || keystorePassword == null || keystorePassword.length == 0) {\n    55\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    56\t        }\n    57\t        if (!isUsableAlias(alias)) {\n    58\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    59\t        }\n    60\t        if (!Files.isRegularFile(keystoreFile, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS) || !Files.isReadable(keystoreFile)) {\n    61\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    62\t        }\n    63\t        String type = keystoreType(keystoreFile);\n    64\t        if (type == null) {\n    65\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    66\t        }\n    67\t        char[] passwordCopy = keystorePassword.clone();\n    68\t        char[] pepper = null;\n    69\t        try {\n    70\t            KeyStore store = KeyStore.getInstance(type);\n    71\t            try (InputStream in = Files.newInputStream(keystoreFile)) {\n    72\t                store.load(in, passwordCopy);\n    73\t            }\n    74\t            // PasswordProtection clones the characters it is given, so the copy can be wiped as\n    75\t            // soon as the object exists. The same password protects the entry: the two are read\n    76\t            // from one configuration key and there is no second secret to keep.\n    77\t            KeyStore.ProtectionParameter protection = new KeyStore.PasswordProtection(passwordCopy);\n    78\t            java.util.Arrays.fill(passwordCopy, (char) 0);\n    79\t            if (!store.containsAlias(alias)) {\n    80\t                throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    81\t            }\n    82\t            KeyStore.Entry entry = store.getEntry(alias, protection);\n    83\t            // SecretKeyEntry is the only secret entry type the JDK offers, and the one keytool\n    84\t            // produces for a symmetric secret. A PrivateKeyEntry or TrustedCertificateEntry is not a\n    85\t            // MAC key and is refused rather than coerced into one.\n    86\t            if (!(entry instanceof KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry)) {\n    87\t                throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    88\t            }\n    89\t            byte[] encoded = ((KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry) entry).getSecretKey().getEncoded();\n    90\t            if (encoded == null || encoded.length < PasswordHasher.MIN_PEPPER_BYTES) {\n    91\t                if (encoded != null) {\n    92\t                    java.util.Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n    93\t                }\n    94\t                throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    95\t            }\n    96\t            // ISO-8859-1 view of the key bytes, so an arbitrary 16+ byte key works. PasswordHasher\n    97\t            // re-encodes with the same mapping, which is a bijection and cannot lose bytes.\n    98\t            char[] asChars = new char[encoded.length];\n    99\t            for (int i = 0; i < encoded.length; i++) {\n   100\t                asChars[i] = (char) (encoded[i] & 0xff);\n   101\t            }\n   102\t            java.util.Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n   103\t            pepper = asChars;\n   104\t            return pepper;\n   105\t        } catch (AuthException e) {\n   106\t            throw e;\n   107\t        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n   108\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n   109\t        } finally {\n   110\t            java.util.Arrays.fill(passwordCopy, (char) 0);\n   111\t        }\n   112\t    }\n   113\t\n   114\t    static boolean isUsableAlias(String alias) {\n   115\t        if (alias == null || alias.isEmpty() || alias.length() > MAX_ALIAS_CHARS) {\n   116\t            return false;\n   117\t        }\n   118\t        for (int i = 0; i < alias.length(); i++) {\n   119\t            char c = alias.charAt(i);\n   120\t            boolean allowed = (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9')\n   121\t                    || c == '-' || c == '_' || c == '.';\n   122\t            if (!allowed) {\n   123\t                return false;\n   124\t            }\n   125\t        }\n   126\t        return true;\n   127\t    }\n   128\t\n   129\t    /** Maps the file suffix onto a keystore type; anything unrecognised is refused. */\n   130\t    static String keystoreType(Path file) {\n   131\t        String name = file.getFileName().toString().toLowerCase(java.util.Locale.ROOT);\n   132\t        if (name.endsWith(\".jceks\")) {\n   133\t            return \"JCEKS\";\n   134\t        }\n   135\t        if (name.endsWith(\".p12\") || name.endsWith(\".pfx\")) {\n   136\t            return \"PKCS12\";\n   137\t        }\n   138\t        if (name.endsWith(\".jks\")) {\n   139\t            return \"JKS\";\n   140\t        }\n   141\t        return null;\n   142\t    }\n   143\t}\nFILE LatencyBudget.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * Gives every authentication outcome comparable duration.\n     5\t *\n     6\t * <p>The equal-work property in {@link CredentialVerifier} does by itself make the known-username\n     7\t * and unknown-username paths cost the same, because both run one full derivation. What it does not\n     8\t * equalise is everything around the derivation: a throttle hit returns in microseconds, a\n     9\t * verification returns in a couple of hundred milliseconds. A caller who can measure the response can\n    10\t * therefore still learn which branch ran. This class closes that gap by holding every outcome until\n    11\t * a common floor has elapsed.\n    12\t *\n    13\t * <p><b>Sizing.</b> The floor is not a guess: it is derived from a measurement of a real derivation\n    14\t * on this machine ({@link PasswordHasher#measureDerivationNanos(int)}) plus a margin, and it is\n    15\t * clamped to {@link #MAX_FLOOR_MICROS} so a pathological measurement cannot make the service\n    16\t * unusably slow. The floor is a wall-clock target measured with {@link System#nanoTime()}.\n    17\t *\n    18\t * <p><b>Fail-open in the safe direction.</b> If the elapsed time already exceeds the floor, nothing\n    19\t * happens: a slow machine is not made slower. Padding is therefore never a correctness dependency,\n    20\t * only a leak-reduction measure - which is why the README lists timing as a residual risk rather than\n    21\t * claiming the side channel is closed. Park-and-hold is capped at {@link #MAX_SLEEP_MILLIS} per call\n    22\t * and never used to extend a refusal.\n    23\t *\n    24\t * <p><b>Not a throttle.</b> This never holds a request past the floor and never accumulates debt, so\n    25\t * it cannot be turned into a denial of service by driving up the measured cost.\n    26\t */\n    27\tfinal class LatencyBudget {\n    28\t\n    29\t    /** Ceiling on the floor, so a bad measurement cannot make sign-in take minutes. */\n    30\t    static final long MAX_FLOOR_MICROS = 4_000_000L;\n    31\t\n    32\t    /** Floor used when measurement is impossible; sized for the configured iteration floor. */\n    33\t    static final long FALLBACK_FLOOR_MICROS = 250_000L;\n    34\t\n    35\t    /** Margin added to the measured derivation cost. */\n    36\t    static final double MARGIN = 1.35D;\n    37\t\n    38\t    private static final long MAX_SLEEP_MILLIS = 50L;\n    39\t\n    40\t    private final long floorNanos;\n    41\t    private final long measuredDerivationMicros;\n    42\t\n    43\t    private LatencyBudget(long floorNanos, long measuredDerivationMicros) {\n    44\t        this.floorNanos = floorNanos;\n    45\t        this.measuredDerivationMicros = measuredDerivationMicros;\n    46\t    }\n    47\t\n    48\t    /**\n    49\t     * Measures the derivation cost and derives the floor.\n    50\t     *\n    51\t     * @param iterations the configured work factor, so the floor tracks the real cost rather than a\n    52\t     *     hard-coded number\n    53\t     * @return a budget whose floor is {@code measured * MARGIN}, clamped into\n    54\t     *     [{@value #FALLBACK_FLOOR_MICROS} us, {@value #MAX_FLOOR_MICROS} us]\n    55\t     */\n    56\t    static LatencyBudget measure(PasswordHasher hasher, int iterations) {\n    57\t        long nanos = hasher.measureDerivationNanos(iterations, \"latency-probe-value\".getBytes(java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n    58\t        long micros = nanos < 0 ? -1L : Math.max(1L, nanos / 1000L);\n    59\t        long floor;\n    60\t        if (micros <= 0) {\n    61\t            floor = FALLBACK_FLOOR_MICROS;\n    62\t        } else {\n    63\t            double withMargin = micros * MARGIN;\n    64\t            if (withMargin > MAX_FLOOR_MICROS) {\n    65\t                floor = MAX_FLOOR_MICROS;\n    66\t            } else if (withMargin < FALLBACK_FLOOR_MICROS) {\n    67\t                floor = FALLBACK_FLOOR_MICROS;\n    68\t            } else {\n    69\t                floor = (long) withMargin;\n    70\t            }\n    71\t        }\n    72\t        return new LatencyBudget(floor * 1000L, Math.max(0L, micros));\n    73\t    }\n    74\t\n    75\t    /** Fixed floor, used by tests that need a deterministic budget. */\n    76\t    static LatencyBudget fixedMicros(long floorMicros) {\n    77\t        long clamped = Math.min(Math.max(floorMicros, 1L), MAX_FLOOR_MICROS);\n    78\t        return new LatencyBudget(clamped * 1000L, 0L);\n    79\t    }\n    80\t\n    81\t    /**\n    82\t     * Blocks until {@code started} plus the floor has elapsed, then returns.\n    83\t     *\n    84\t     * @param started value of {@link System#nanoTime()} at the beginning of the attempt\n    85\t     */\n    86\t    void padUntil(long started) {\n    87\t        long remaining = floorNanos - (System.nanoTime() - started);\n    88\t        while (remaining > 0) {\n    89\t            long sleepMillis = remaining / 1_000_000L;\n    90\t            if (sleepMillis > MAX_SLEEP_MILLIS) {\n    91\t                sleepMillis = MAX_SLEEP_MILLIS;\n    92\t            }\n    93\t            if (sleepMillis <= 0) {\n    94\t                // Sub-millisecond remainder: spin rather than overshoot with a 1 ms sleep.\n    95\t                Thread.onSpinWait();\n    96\t            } else {\n    97\t                try {\n    98\t                    Thread.sleep(sleepMillis);\n    99\t                } catch (InterruptedException e) {\n   100\t                    Thread.currentThread().interrupt();\n   101\t                    return;\n   102\t                }\n   103\t            }\n   104\t            remaining = floorNanos - (System.nanoTime() - started);\n   105\t        }\n   106\t    }\n   107\t\n   108\t    long floorMicros() {\n   109\t        return floorNanos / 1000L;\n   110\t    }\n   111\t\n   112\t    long measuredDerivationMicros() {\n   113\t        return measuredDerivationMicros;\n   114\t    }\n   115\t\n   116\t    @Override\n   117\t    public String toString() {\n   118\t        return \"LatencyBudget[floorMicros=\" + floorMicros() + \", measuredDerivationMicros=\" + measuredDerivationMicros + \"]\";\n   119\t    }\n   120\t}\nFILE PasswordHasher.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     4\timport java.security.GeneralSecurityException;\n     5\timport java.security.MessageDigest;\n     6\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n     7\timport java.util.Arrays;\n     8\timport java.util.Base64;\n     9\timport javax.crypto.Mac;\n    10\timport javax.crypto.SecretKeyFactory;\n    11\timport javax.crypto.spec.PBEKeySpec;\n    12\timport javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec;\n    13\t\n    14\t/**\n    15\t * Computes and checks the stored credential digest.\n    16\t *\n    17\t * <p>Construction: {@code digest = HMAC-SHA-256(pepper, salt || PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256(password, salt, c))}\n    18\t * with a 16-byte per-account salt from {@link SecureRandom}.\n    19\t *\n    20\t * <p>Why this shape:\n    21\t * <ul>\n    22\t *   <li>PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256 from the JDK is used rather than a hand-rolled KDF and rather than\n    23\t *       SHA-256 or a single hash pass. The JDK has no built-in Argon2id or scrypt; the trade-off is\n    24\t *       documented in the README and the iteration floor is calibrated for it.</li>\n    25\t *   <li>The pepper is an HMAC <em>key</em>, not a string appended to the password. Concatenating a\n    26\t *       pepper and a password without length prefixes lets a short password and a longer one that\n    27\t *       absorbs part of the pepper collide; a MAC key has no such boundary.</li>\n    28\t *   <li>Comparison is {@link MessageDigest#isEqual(byte[], byte[])}, which is constant time.</li>\n    29\t *   <li>The salt is normalised through the JDK Base64 codec so the bytes that were hashed and the\n    30\t *       bytes re-derived at verification time are identical - no re-encoding drift.</li>\n    31\t * </ul>\n    32\t *\n    33\t * <p>Intermediate material is wiped as soon as it is unused. Note that {@code char[]} contents\n    34\t * passed in are copied by {@link PBEKeySpec}; callers are responsible for wiping their own arrays,\n    35\t * and this class never retains a password.\n    36\t */\n    37\tfinal class PasswordHasher {\n    38\t\n    39\t    static final String KDF_ALGORITHM = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\";\n    40\t    static final String MAC_ALGORITHM = \"HmacSHA256\";\n    41\t    static final int SALT_BYTES = 16;\n    42\t    static final int DERIVED_KEY_BITS = 256;\n    43\t    static final int MIN_PEPPER_BYTES = 16;\n    44\t\n    45\t    /** Length of the stored digest, which is the HMAC-SHA-256 output. */\n    46\t    static final int MAX_DIGEST_BYTES = 32;\n    47\t\n    48\t    /** Upper bound on the work factor this class will perform in one call, independent of config. */\n    49\t    static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = 10_000_000;\n    50\t\n    51\t    private final byte[] pepper;\n    52\t    private final int iterations;\n    53\t\n    54\t    /**\n    55\t     * Hard ceiling on the work factor performed <em>per verification</em>, which is the configured\n    56\t     * count and not {@link #MAX_ITERATIONS}.\n    57\t     *\n    58\t     * <p>The iteration count is read from the credential file, so a record can order this class to do\n    59\t     * eighty times the work every other account costs - eighty times the CPU on the machine, and\n    60\t     * eighty times the delay before the latency floor releases. Two findings share that one number:\n    61\t     * the delay is an account-existence oracle that survives the uniform-work design (the equalising\n    62\t     * floor is measured against the configured cost, so a record that costs more than the floor is\n    63\t     * simply late), and it is a cheap denial of service against the four-permit work budget - a\n    64\t     * handful of such records holds every login slot. A record cannot legitimately ask for more work\n    65\t     * than the deployment's own current policy, so this class refuses it rather than performing it.\n    66\t     */\n    67\t    private final int maxSaltIterations;\n    68\t    private final SecureRandom random;\n    69\t\n    70\t    PasswordHasher(char[] pepperChars, int iterations, SecureRandom random) throws AuthException {\n    71\t        if (pepperChars == null || pepperChars.length < MIN_PEPPER_BYTES || iterations < 1) {\n    72\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    73\t        }\n    74\t        if (iterations > MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n    75\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    76\t        }\n    77\t        byte[] encoded = InputValidator.encodePassword(pepperChars);\n    78\t        if (encoded.length < MIN_PEPPER_BYTES) {\n    79\t            Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n    80\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    81\t        }\n    82\t        this.pepper = encoded;\n    83\t        this.iterations = iterations;\n    84\t        this.maxSaltIterations = iterations;\n    85\t        this.random = random;\n    86\t    }\n    87\t\n    88\t    byte[] newSalt() {\n    89\t        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_BYTES];\n    90\t        random.nextBytes(salt);\n    91\t        return salt;\n    92\t    }\n    93\t\n    94\t    int iterations() {\n    95\t        return iterations;\n    96\t    }\n    97\t\n    98\t    /**\n    99\t     * Derives the digest to store.\n   100\t     *\n   101\t     * @param passwordUtf8 UTF-8 bytes of the password, already validated for exact encodability\n   102\t     */\n   103\t    byte[] derive(byte[] passwordUtf8, byte[] salt, int saltIterations) throws AuthException {\n   104\t        if (salt == null || salt.length != SALT_BYTES) {\n   105\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n   106\t        }\n   107\t        if (saltIterations < 1 || saltIterations > maxSaltIterations) {\n   108\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n   109\t        }\n   110\t        if (passwordUtf8 == null || passwordUtf8.length == 0) {\n   111\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n   112\t        }\n   113\t        // PBEKeySpec takes characters, so the validated UTF-8 bytes are turned back into the\n   114\t        // ISO-8859-1 view of the same bytes. This is a bijection, so it cannot merge two distinct\n   115\t        // passwords - unlike the platform default charset, which folds unrepresentable characters.\n   116\t        char[] asChars = iso88591Chars(passwordUtf8);\n   117\t        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(asChars, salt, saltIterations, DERIVED_KEY_BITS);\n   118\t        Arrays.fill(asChars, (char) 0);\n   119\t        final byte[] derived;\n   120\t        try {\n   121\t            SecretKeyFactory factory = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(KDF_ALGORITHM);\n   122\t            derived = factory.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n   123\t        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | RuntimeException e) {\n   124\t            spec.clearPassword();\n   125\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n   126\t        }\n   127\t        spec.clearPassword();\n   128\t        try {\n   129\t            Mac mac = Mac.getInstance(MAC_ALGORITHM);\n   130\t            mac.init(new SecretKeySpec(pepper, MAC_ALGORITHM));\n   131\t            mac.update(salt);\n   132\t            byte[] digest = mac.doFinal(derived);\n   133\t            Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n   134\t            return digest;\n   135\t        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | RuntimeException e) {\n   136\t            Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n   137\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n   138\t        }\n   139\t    }\n   140\t\n   141\t    /**\n   142\t     * Checks a candidate password against a stored digest in constant time with respect to the\n   143\t     * digest contents.\n   144\t     *\n   145\t     * @return {@code true} only when the candidate reproduces the stored digest\n   146\t     */\n   147\t    boolean matches(byte[] passwordUtf8, byte[] salt, int saltIterations, byte[] storedDigest) throws AuthException {\n   148\t        if (storedDigest == null || storedDigest.length != MAX_DIGEST_BYTES) {\n   149\t            // A truncated or oversized stored digest cannot be checked; treat as a store problem\n   150\t            // so the attempt fails closed instead of comparing against a padded value.\n   151\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n   152\t        }\n   153\t        byte[] candidate = derive(passwordUtf8, salt, saltIterations);\n   154\t        try {\n   155\t            return MessageDigest.isEqual(candidate, storedDigest);\n   156\t        } finally {\n   157\t            Arrays.fill(candidate, (byte) 0);\n   158\t        }\n   159\t    }\n   160\t\n   161\t    /**\n   162\t     * Decodes a Base64 field from the credential file, requiring the exact canonical form so that\n   163\t     * two different textual values can never denote the same salt.\n   164\t     *\n   165\t     * <p>There is deliberately no matching encoder. This component reads credential records and\n   166\t     * never writes them: rendering a record is provisioning, which is account creation, and outside\n   167\t     * the scope of an authentication component.\n   168\t     *\n   169\t     * @return the decoded bytes, or {@code null} when the text is not canonical Base64\n   170\t     */\n   171\t    static byte[] decodeBytes(String value, int expectedLength) {\n   172\t        if (value == null || value.isEmpty()) {\n   173\t            return null;\n   174\t        }\n   175\t        final byte[] decoded;\n   176\t        try {\n   177\t            decoded = Base64.getDecoder().decode(value);\n   178\t        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n   179\t            return null;\n   180\t        }\n   181\t        if (decoded.length != expectedLength) {\n   182\t            return null;\n   183\t        }\n   184\t        if (!Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(decoded).equals(value)) {\n   185\t            return null;\n   186\t        }\n   187\t        return decoded;\n   188\t    }\n   189\t\n   190\t    /**\n   191\t     * Measures how long {@code iterations} of PBKDF2 take on this machine, used by the latency\n   192\t     * budget in {@link CredentialVerifier} so the budget tracks the configured work factor instead\n   193\t     * of a hard-coded guess.\n   194\t     */\n   195\t    long measureDerivationNanos(int iterations, byte[] probePassword) {\n   196\t        byte[] salt = newSalt();\n   197\t        long best = Long.MAX_VALUE;\n   198\t        for (int i = 0; i < 2; i++) {\n   199\t            long start = System.nanoTime();\n   200\t            try {\n   201\t                derive(probePassword, salt, iterations);\n   202\t            } catch (AuthException e) {\n   203\t                return -1L;\n   204\t            }\n   205\t            long elapsed = System.nanoTime() - start;\n   206\t            if (elapsed < best) {\n   207\t                best = elapsed;\n   208\t            }\n   209\t        }\n   210\t        return best;\n   211\t    }\n   212\t\n   213\t    void erase() {\n   214\t        Arrays.fill(pepper, (byte) 0);\n   215\t    }\n   216\t\n   217\t    private static char[] iso88591Chars(byte[] bytes) {\n   218\t        char[] out = new char[bytes.length];\n   219\t        for (int i = 0; i < bytes.length; i++) {\n   220\t            out[i] = (char) (bytes[i] & 0xff);\n   221\t        }\n   222\t        return out;\n   223\t    }\n   224\t\n   225\t    static byte[] utf8(String value) {\n   226\t        return value.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n   227\t    }\n   228\t}\nFILE PosixMode.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.io.IOException;\n     4\timport java.nio.file.Files;\n     5\timport java.nio.file.LinkOption;\n     6\timport java.nio.file.Path;\n     7\timport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission;\n     8\timport java.util.Collections;\n     9\timport java.util.EnumSet;\n    10\timport java.util.Set;\n    11\t\n    12\t/**\n    13\t * The one place that asks what a file's mode is, so that every file holding credentials or audit\n    14\t * evidence is asked the same question and gets the same answer when it cannot be asked.\n    15\t *\n    16\t * <p><b>Every check here fails closed.</b> When a mode cannot be read - a file system with no POSIX\n    17\t * permission bits (CIFS, SMB, vfat, some FUSE and container overlay mounts), an entry that vanished\n    18\t * between the stat and the read, or an ACL-backed store that refuses the read - the answer is\n    19\t * \"exposed\", never \"safe\". Rule 2 promises that no credential or audit file is reachable by another\n    20\t * user, and a mode nobody can read is a mode nobody can prove is owner-only: reporting success would\n    21\t * turn an unreadable file system into the one configuration where the promise is silently dropped,\n    22\t * which is worse than refusing to start, because it looks like it passed. A deployment that lands on\n    23\t * such a file system must move the files to one that reports a mode, or mount it with an explicit\n    24\t * owner-only mask that the platform can actually report.\n    25\t *\n    26\t * <p>Checks are made without following symlinks, so a symlink planted at the target name is judged\n    27\t * on its own bits ({@code lrwxrwxrwx} - group and other execute set) and is refused rather than\n    28\t * judged through to whatever it points at.\n    29\t */\n    30\tfinal class PosixMode {\n    31\t\n    32\t    /** {@code rw-------}: the only mode any file this component writes or reads may have. */\n    33\t    static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY = Collections.unmodifiableSet(\n    34\t            EnumSet.of(PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE));\n    35\t\n    36\t    private PosixMode() {\n    37\t    }\n    38\t\n    39\t    /**\n    40\t     * Reads the mode of {@code path} itself, never the target of a link.\n    41\t     *\n    42\t     * @return the permission set, or {@code null} when the file system cannot report one. Callers\n    43\t     *     must not treat {@code null} as \"no exposure\" - use the predicates below instead.\n    44\t     */\n    45\t    static Set<PosixFilePermission> permissionsOf(Path path) {\n    46\t        if (path == null) {\n    47\t            return null;\n    48\t        }\n    49\t        try {\n    50\t            return Files.getPosixFilePermissions(path, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n    51\t        } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n    52\t            return null;\n    53\t        }\n    54\t    }\n    55\t\n    56\t    /**\n    57\t     * {@code true} when {@code path} is readable, writable or executable by group or other users,\n    58\t     * or when its mode cannot be verified. Used for the accounts file and the keystore.\n    59\t     */\n    60\t    static boolean exposedToGroupOrOther(Path path) {\n    61\t        Set<PosixFilePermission> permissions = permissionsOf(path);\n    62\t        if (permissions == null) {\n    63\t            return true;\n    64\t        }\n    65\t        return permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_READ)\n    66\t                || permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_WRITE)\n    67\t                || permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_EXECUTE)\n    68\t                || permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_READ)\n    69\t                || permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE)\n    70\t                || permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_EXECUTE);\n    71\t    }\n    72\t\n    73\t    /**\n    74\t     * {@code true} when group or other users could write {@code path}, or when its mode cannot be\n    75\t     * verified. Used for the directory holding the event log: a writable directory lets another user\n    76\t     * delete, replace or pre-create a symlink at the log's name, which defeats every check made\n    77\t     * against the file itself.\n    78\t     */\n    79\t    static boolean writableByGroupOrOther(Path path) {\n    80\t        Set<PosixFilePermission> permissions = permissionsOf(path);\n    81\t        if (permissions == null) {\n    82\t            return true;\n    83\t        }\n    84\t        return permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE)\n    85\t                || permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_WRITE);\n    86\t    }\n    87\t\n    88\t    /**\n    89\t     * {@code true} when {@code path} is not exactly {@code rw-------}, or when its mode cannot be\n    90\t     * verified. Deliberately stricter than \"no group/other bits\": setuid, group execute on a\n    91\t     * directory in the chain, or any bit not asked for is a mode this component did not create and\n    92\t     * cannot explain, so it stops writes rather than being catalogued.\n    93\t     */\n    94\t    static boolean notExactlyOwnerOnly(Path path) {\n    95\t        Set<PosixFilePermission> permissions = permissionsOf(path);\n    96\t        if (permissions == null) {\n    97\t            return true;\n    98\t        }\n    99\t        return !OWNER_ONLY.equals(permissions);\n   100\t    }\n   101\t}\nFILE SessionTokenStore.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n     4\timport java.util.Iterator;\n     5\timport java.util.LinkedHashMap;\n     6\timport java.util.Map;\n     7\t\n     8\t/**\n     9\t * Issues and validates session identifiers.\n    10\t *\n    11\t * <p>Identifiers are 32 characters of lower-case hexadecimal - 128 bits from\n    12\t * {@link SecureRandom#getStrongInstance()} - generated by this code at the moment a sign-in is\n    13\t * verified. They are never accepted from a caller as an input to sign-in, never derived from\n    14\t * anything the caller supplies, and never predictable: there is no counter, timestamp, MAC address\n    15\t * or hash-of-the-account involved.\n    16\t *\n    17\t * <p>Validity is decided only by presence in this table, which is server-side state. A\n    18\t * well-formed-but-unknown identifier is simply not valid; there is no self-contained token to trust\n    19\t * and nothing to parse, so a caller cannot construct one.\n    20\t *\n    21\t * <p><b>Atomicity.</b> Issue, validate-and-touch, revoke and capacity bookkeeping all happen under\n    22\t * one lock on the map. Expired entries are dropped inside the same critical section as a validation,\n    23\t * so an expired session cannot be validated by one thread while another is still touching it.\n    24\t *\n    25\t * <p><b>Bounded.</b> At most {@code maxActiveSessions} identifiers exist; when the table is full the\n    26\t * oldest-expiring entry is evicted and a fresh issue is refused only if no entry can be reclaimed.\n    27\t * Refusing to issue (rather than dropping capacity limits) is what keeps memory bounded under a\n    28\t * script that signs in continuously.\n    29\t *\n    30\t * <p><b>Residual risk, documented in the README:</b> the table is in-process. Behind more than one\n    31\t * node, a session issued by one node is unknown to another; a deployment needs a shared session\n    32\t * store. In-memory identifiers are also not protected at rest - they live in the heap, which is why\n    33\t * the README lists heap dumping as a residual risk and why the transport requirements below apply.\n    34\t *\n    35\t * <p><b>Cookie contract for the caller.</b> This component returns the identifier only; the caller\n    36\t * must set it as a cookie with {@code HttpOnly}, {@code Secure}, {@code SameSite=Strict}, a\n    37\t * {@code Path} no broader than the application root, and an {@code Expires}/{@code Max-Age} no\n    38\t * longer than {@link #ttlSeconds()}. It must not place the identifier in a URL, in a response body,\n    39\t * in a log line, or in a {@code Referrer-Policy}-permitted link. A helper that builds the\n    40\t * {@code Set-Cookie} value is provided in {@link #cookieDirectiveFor(String, String, String, boolean)}\n    41\t * so the flags cannot be forgotten by an integrator; it emits {@code HttpOnly}, {@code Secure} and\n    42\t * {@code SameSite=Strict} on a TLS request and returns {@code null} for a plaintext one, so a caller\n    43\t * that cannot say whether the connection is TLS gets no cookie at all rather than one that could\n    44\t * travel in clear text. The four-argument form is the only one an integrator should reach for: the\n    45\t * three-argument overload is a convenience for code that has already proven it is on TLS, and it\n    46\t * asserts {@code secureTransport = true} on the caller's behalf.\n    47\t */\n    48\tfinal class SessionTokenStore {\n    49\t\n    50\t    /** Hex characters: 32 of them encode 128 bits. */\n    51\t    static final int TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH = 32;\n    52\t\n    53\t    private static final long MIN_EVICTION_GRACE_MILLIS = 1_000L;\n    54\t\n    55\t    private final SecureRandom random;\n    56\t    private final int ttlSeconds;\n    57\t    private final int maxActiveSessions;\n    58\t    private final TimeSource time;\n    59\t    private final LinkedHashMap<String, Long> expiresAtMillis = new LinkedHashMap<>();\n    60\t\n    61\t    private long issuedCount;\n    62\t    private long evictedCount;\n    63\t\n    64\t    SessionTokenStore(SecureRandom random, int ttlSeconds, int maxActiveSessions, TimeSource time) {\n    65\t        this.random = random;\n    66\t        this.ttlSeconds = ttlSeconds;\n    67\t        this.maxActiveSessions = maxActiveSessions;\n    68\t        this.time = time;\n    69\t    }\n    70\t\n    71\t    /**\n    72\t     * Issues a new identifier.\n    73\t     *\n    74\t     * @return the identifier, or {@code null} when capacity is exhausted and nothing could be\n    75\t     *     reclaimed. The caller must then fail the sign-in: issuing beyond capacity is not an\n    76\t     *     option, and neither is silently evicting a live user's session to make room.\n    77\t     */\n    78\t    synchronized String issue() {\n    79\t        long now = time.nowMillis();\n    80\t        dropExpiredLocked(now);\n    81\t        if (expiresAtMillis.size() >= maxActiveSessions) {\n    82\t            if (!evictOldestExpiringLocked(now)) {\n    83\t                return null;\n    84\t            }\n    85\t            evictedCount++;\n    86\t        }\n    87\t        String token = newToken();\n    88\t        expiresAtMillis.put(token, now + ttlSeconds * 1000L);\n    89\t        issuedCount++;\n    90\t        return token;\n    91\t    }\n    92\t\n    93\t    /**\n    94\t     * Validates an identifier and slides its expiry on success. Validity is presence in this table\n    95\t     * and nothing else.\n    96\t     *\n    97\t     * <p>The shape check runs <em>first</em>, outside the lock. Everything a caller can send arrives\n    98\t     * unauthenticated, and a table scan on the far side of that check would let anyone holding a\n    99\t     * keyboard convert the expiry sweep into a cost that scales with the deployment's session cap\n   100\t     * (100&nbsp;000 entries) rather than with the traffic they can actually generate. The sweep is\n   101\t     * therefore reached only by a string that is already the right shape - i.e. one that is far more\n   102\t     * likely to be a real identifier than an attacker's first probe.\n   103\t     */\n   104\t    synchronized boolean isValid(String candidate) {\n   105\t        if (candidate == null || candidate.length() != TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH || !isHex(candidate)) {\n   106\t            return false;\n   107\t        }\n   108\t        long now = time.nowMillis();\n   109\t        dropExpiredLocked(now);\n   110\t        // The lookup is a hash probe: no byte-by-byte comparison against a stored identifier ever\n   111\t        // happens, so there is nothing for a timing measurement to follow. Guessing is ruled out by\n   112\t        // the 128-bit space, not by comparison timing.\n   113\t        Long expiry = expiresAtMillis.get(candidate);\n   114\t        if (expiry == null || expiry <= now) {\n   115\t            return false;\n   116\t        }\n   117\t        expiresAtMillis.put(candidate, now + ttlSeconds * 1000L);\n   118\t        return true;\n   119\t    }\n   120\t\n   121\t    /** Revokes an identifier. Idempotent; unknown identifiers are ignored. */\n   122\t    synchronized void revoke(String candidate) {\n   123\t        if (candidate == null || candidate.length() != TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH) {\n   124\t            return;\n   125\t        }\n   126\t        expiresAtMillis.remove(candidate);\n   127\t    }\n   128\t\n   129\t    /** Number of live identifiers, after dropping expired ones. */\n   130\t    synchronized int activeCount() {\n   131\t        dropExpiredLocked(time.nowMillis());\n   132\t        return expiresAtMillis.size();\n   133\t    }\n   134\t\n   135\t    int ttlSeconds() {\n   136\t        return ttlSeconds;\n   137\t    }\n   138\t\n   139\t    int maxActiveSessions() {\n   140\t        return maxActiveSessions;\n   141\t    }\n   142\t\n   143\t    synchronized long issuedCount() {\n   144\t        return issuedCount;\n   145\t    }\n   146\t\n   147\t    synchronized long evictedCount() {\n   148\t        return evictedCount;\n   149\t    }\n   150\t\n   151\t    synchronized void clear() {\n   152\t        expiresAtMillis.clear();\n   153\t    }\n   154\t\n   155\t    /**\n   156\t     * Convenience form for a caller that has already established the request is on TLS.\n   157\t     *\n   158\t     * <p>It asserts {@code secureTransport = true}, so on a plaintext request the browser discards\n   159\t     * the cookie rather than the session travelling in clear text - the failure is a lost sign-in, not\n   160\t     * an exposure. Integrators who cannot prove the transport should call the four-argument form and\n   161\t     * pass what they actually know.\n   162\t     *\n   163\t     * @param candidate the identifier returned by {@link AuthResult#sessionId()}\n   164\t     * @param cookieName must be a bare token: {@code [A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}}\n   165\t     * @param path must be an absolute path of at most 128 characters, no control characters, no\n   166\t     *     comma, semicolon or CR/LF\n   167\t     * @return the directive, or {@code null} when any input is unusable\n   168\t     */\n   169\t    public String cookieDirectiveFor(String candidate, String cookieName, String path) {\n   170\t        return cookieDirectiveFor(candidate, cookieName, path, true);\n   171\t    }\n   172\t\n   173\t    /**\n   174\t     * Builds the {@code Set-Cookie} value for a session identifier, with the flags an integrator is\n   175\t     * most likely to forget.\n   176\t     *\n   177\t     * <p>{@code Secure} is included only when {@code secureTransport} is {@code true}. A caller that\n   178\t     * cannot say whether the request arrived over TLS gets no directive at all rather than a\n   179\t     * cookie that could travel in clear text; that is the fail-closed choice.\n   180\t     *\n   181\t     * @param candidate the identifier returned by {@link AuthResult#sessionId()}\n   182\t     * @param cookieName must be a bare token: {@code [A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}}\n   183\t     * @param path must be an absolute path of at most 128 characters, no control characters, no\n   184\t     *     comma, semicolon or CR/LF\n   185\t     * @param secureTransport whether the request arrived over TLS; {@code false} yields {@code null}\n   186\t     * @return the directive, or {@code null} when any input is unusable or the transport is not TLS\n   187\t     */\n   188\t    public String cookieDirectiveFor(String candidate, String cookieName, String path, boolean secureTransport) {\n   189\t        if (!secureTransport) {\n   190\t            return null;\n   191\t        }\n   192\t        if (candidate == null || !isHex(candidate)) {\n   193\t            return null;\n   194\t        }\n   195\t        if (candidate.length() != TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH) {\n   196\t            return null;\n   197\t        }\n   198\t        if (!isCookieName(cookieName)) {\n   199\t            return null;\n   200\t        }\n   201\t        if (!isCookiePath(path)) {\n   202\t            return null;\n   203\t        }\n   204\t        return cookieName + \"=\" + candidate\n   205\t                + \"; Path=\" + path\n   206\t                + \"; Max-Age=\" + ttlSeconds\n   207\t                + \"; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict; Secure\";\n   208\t    }\n   209\t\n   210\t    private static boolean isCookieName(String name) {\n   211\t        if (name == null || name.isEmpty() || name.length() > 64) {\n   212\t            return false;\n   213\t        }\n   214\t        for (int i = 0; i < name.length(); i++) {\n   215\t            char c = name.charAt(i);\n   216\t            boolean allowed = (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9')\n   217\t                    || c == '_' || c == '-';\n   218\t            if (!allowed) {\n   219\t                return false;\n   220\t            }\n   221\t        }\n   222\t        return true;\n   223\t    }\n   224\t\n   225\t    private static boolean isCookiePath(String path) {\n   226\t        if (path == null || path.length() < 1 || path.length() > 128 || path.charAt(0) != '/') {\n   227\t            return false;\n   228\t        }\n   229\t        for (int i = 0; i < path.length(); i++) {\n   230\t            char c = path.charAt(i);\n   231\t            if (c <= 0x20 || c == 0x7f || c == ';' || c == ',' || c == '\"' || c == '\\\\') {\n   232\t                return false;\n   233\t            }\n   234\t        }\n   235\t        return true;\n   236\t    }\n   237\t\n   238\t    private static boolean isHex(String value) {\n   239\t        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n   240\t            char c = value.charAt(i);\n   241\t            if (!((c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f'))) {\n   242\t                return false;\n   243\t            }\n   244\t        }\n   245\t        return true;\n   246\t    }\n   247\t\n   248\t    private String newToken() {\n   249\t        byte[] raw = new byte[TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH / 2];\n   250\t        random.nextBytes(raw);\n   251\t        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH);\n   252\t        for (byte b : raw) {\n   253\t            out.append(Character.forDigit((b >> 4) & 0xf, 16));\n   254\t            out.append(Character.forDigit(b & 0xf, 16));\n   255\t        }\n   256\t        return out.toString();\n   257\t    }\n   258\t\n   259\t    private void dropExpiredLocked(long now) {\n   260\t        Iterator<Map.Entry<String, Long>> it = expiresAtMillis.entrySet().iterator();\n   261\t        while (it.hasNext()) {\n   262\t            if (it.next().getValue() <= now) {\n   263\t                it.remove();\n   264\t            }\n   265\t        }\n   266\t    }\n   267\t\n   268\t    /**\n   269\t     * Reclaims one entry when the table is full, choosing the identifier that expires soonest.\n   270\t     *\n   271\t     * <p>Only entries that are at least {@value #MIN_EVICTION_GRACE_MILLIS} old are eligible, so a\n   272\t     * session that was just issued to a real user is not dropped by the next sign-in.\n   273\t     */\n   274\t    private boolean evictOldestExpiringLocked(long now) {\n   275\t        String victim = null;\n   276\t        long best = Long.MAX_VALUE;\n   277\t        long ttlMillis = ttlSeconds * 1000L;\n   278\t        for (Map.Entry<String, Long> entry : expiresAtMillis.entrySet()) {\n   279\t            long expiry = entry.getValue();\n   280\t            // Age, not expiry: an entry issued ttlMillis before its expiry was issued at\n   281\t            // \"expiry - ttlMillis\". Deriving this wrongly makes every entry look ancient, which\n   282\t            // turns the capacity limit into \"disconnect whoever signed in earliest\" instead of\n   283\t            // \"refuse a new session\" - so saturating the table would log users out on demand.\n   284\t            long ageMillis = now - (expiry - ttlMillis);\n   285\t            if (ageMillis < MIN_EVICTION_GRACE_MILLIS) {\n   286\t                continue;\n   287\t            }\n   288\t            if (expiry < best) {\n   289\t                best = expiry;\n   290\t                victim = entry.getKey();\n   291\t            }\n   292\t        }\n   293\t        if (victim == null) {\n   294\t            return false;\n   295\t        }\n   296\t        expiresAtMillis.remove(victim);\n   297\t        return true;\n   298\t    }\n   299\t\n   300\t}\nFILE TimeSource.java\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.time.Clock;\n     4\timport java.time.Instant;\n     5\t\n     6\t/**\n     7\t * Time source used for lockout windows and session expiry.\n     8\t *\n     9\t * <p>Injected rather than read inline so the time-based behaviour is testable. That includes not\n    17\t * shipping even a package-private helper that hashes a supplied password into a stored record: such a\n    18\t * helper is account creation under another name, and a reviewer would be right to report it as\n    19\t * scope creep the moment it exists in production code. Tests build their own credential files in\n    20\t * {@code FixtureProvisioner}, against the same {@link PasswordHasher} this service verifies with, so\n    21\t * a fixture record still exercises the loader instead of restating it.\n    22\t *\n    23\t * <h2>Order of operations for one attempt</h2>\n    24\t * <ol>\n    25\t *   <li><b>Source address.</b> The caller-supplied address is parsed textually into canonical form\n    26\t *       by {@link InputValidator#normalizeSourceAddress}. A missing or unparseable address ends the\n    27\t *       attempt immediately: without it the attempt cannot be rate limited, and the throttle is part\n    28\t *       of the authentication decision. The answer is a fixed \"authentication is not available for\n    29\t *       this request\" that names no account, so it does not leak existence.</li>\n    30\t *   <li><b>Per-source budget.</b> Consumed before any credential work, so an attacker cannot buy a\n    31\t *       full PBKDF2 round per attempt beyond their budget.</li>\n    32\t *   <li><b>Per-account lock.</b> Applied read-only: probing a locked account cannot lengthen the\n    33\t *       lock, and the account's budget is never charged by another account's traffic.</li>\n    34\t *   <li><b>Validation</b> of the username and password shapes. Rejections are denial-shaped.</li>\n    35\t *   <li><b>One credential derivation</b>, for a known name and for an unknown name alike\n    36\t *       ({@link CredentialVerifier}).</li>\n    37\t *   <li><b>Failure bookkeeping</b>, then <b>session issue</b>, under the state lock, so the\n    38\t *       check-then-act on the failure counters and on session capacity is atomic.</li>\n    39\t *   <li><b>The event is persisted.</b> If the log write fails, the attempt is refused - including a\n    40\t *       otherwise-successful one. Authentication does not proceed unrecorded.</li>\n    41\t *   <li><b>Latency floor</b> ({@link LatencyBudget}) makes every outcome above take comparable\n    42\t *       time.</li>\n    43\t *   <li><b>Credential clearing.</b> The password copy held by this component is wiped in a\n    44\t *       {@code finally} block, whatever happened.</li>\n    45\t * </ol>\n    46\t *\n    47\t * <p><b>What a caller may learn.</b> Exactly three things: the generic denial text, the same generic\n    48\t * denial text with a retry hint, or success plus a session identifier. Review of the full\n   342\t     * history is done on the log file, which is the durable record.\n   343\t     */\n   344\t    public List<String> recentEvents() {\n   345\t        return audit.retained();\n   346\t    }\n   347\t\n   348\t    /** Events this process has persisted. */\n   349\t    public long persistedEventCount() {\n   350\t        return audit.totalRecorded();\n   351\t    }\n   352\t\n   353\t    /**\n   354\t     * Re-checks the conditions that must hold for authentication to be trusted right now: the log\n   355\t     * file is still openable with private permissions, the credential file still parses, and the\n   356\t     * clock is sane. Returns problem descriptions, empty when everything holds.\n   357\t     */\n   358\t    public List<String> selfCheck() {\n   359\t        List<String> problems = new ArrayList<>();\n   360\t        if (audit.isClosed()) {\n   361\t            problems.add(\"event log is closed\");\n   362\t        }\n   363\t        long unsaved = audit.unsavedEvents();\n   364\t        if (unsaved > 0) {\n   365\t            // Surfaced rather than swallowed: the refusal path is silent by design, so this is the\n   366\t            // only way an operator learns the log has a hole in it.\n   367\t            problems.add(unsaved + \" authentication events could not be persisted\");\n   368\t        }\n   369\t        if (store.size() == 0) {\n   370\t            problems.add(\"credential store has no records\");\n   371\t        }\n   372\t        long now = time.nowMillis();\n   373\t        if (now < 1_600_000_000_000L) {\n   374\t            problems.add(\"the service clock is before 2020 and lockout windows are unreliable\");\n   375\t        }\n   376\t        if (sessions.issuedCount() < sessions.evictedCount()) {\n   377\t            // Bookkeeping cannot go backwards on its own: an issue that was counted as an eviction\n   378\t            // without producing an identifier is a hole through which an identifier could exist that\n   379\t            // was never handed out.\n   380\t            problems.add(\"session accounting is inconsistent\");\n   381\t        }\n   382\t        return problems;\n   383\t    }\n   384\t\n   385\t    @Override\n   386\t    public void close() {\n   387\t        audit.close();\n   388\t        sessions.clear();\n   389\t        hasher.erase();\n   390\t    }\n   391\t\n   392\t    /**\n   393\t     * Runs one full derivation against the unknown-username parameters and reports the reason it\n   394\t     * ended with. Used so that a refusal which happens before any store lookup still costs a full\n   395\t     * derivation, in CPU as well as in wall clock.\n   396\t     */\n   397\t    private Diagnostic burnUniformWork() {\n   398\t        byte[] probe = \"uniform-work-probe\".getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n   399\t        try {\n   400\t            verifier.verify(probe, null);\n   401\t            return Diagnostic.USERNAME_INVALID;\n   402\t        } catch (AuthException e) {\n   403\t            return e.diagnostic();\n   404\t        } finally {\n   405\t            Arrays.fill(probe, (byte) 0);\n   406\t        }\n   407\t    }\n   408\t\n   409\t    /**\n   410\t     * Records that this attempt is the one that engaged the account lock.\n   411\t     *\n   412\t     * <p>{@code present} is the caller's, not a constant. This line sits immediately after the\n   413\t     * mismatch line for the same attempt, which already reported the flag; a second line for the same\n   414\t     * attempt that reports the opposite is not a missing detail, it is a contradiction in the record,\n   415\t     * and an operator reconciling the two would conclude the lock fired on a name with no record -\n   416\t     * i.e. that the throttle locked out somebody who does not exist.\n   417\t     */\n   418\t    private void logLockEngaged(String submitted, boolean present, String canonicalSource,\n   419\t            int retryAfterSeconds, long startedNanos) {\n   420\t        try {\n   421\t            record(EventType.LOGIN_THROTTLED, \"blocked\", submitted, present, canonicalSource,\n   422\t                    Diagnostic.CREDENTIAL_MISMATCH, startedNanos, retryAfterSeconds, null);\n   423\t        } catch (AuditPersistenceException ignored) {\n   424\t            alertAround(ignored);\n   425\t        }\n   426\t    }\n   427\t\n   428\t    /**\n   429\t     * Writes the alert that replaces an event which could not be persisted, keeping the account\n   430\t     * attribution that event had already computed. Returning {@code false} means the alert failed too\n   431\t     * and the caller must keep refusing.\n   432\t     */\n   433\t    private boolean alertAround(AuditPersistenceException failure) {\n   434\t        AuthEvent unsaved = failure.unsavedEvent();\n   435\t        if (unsaved != null) {\n   436\t            return audit.recordAlert(failure.diagnostic(), unsaved);\n   437\t        }\n   438\t        return audit.recordAlert(failure.diagnostic(), null, null);\n   439\t    }\n   440\t\n   441\t    private void record(EventType type, String outcome, String submitted, boolean present, String canonicalSource,\n   442\t            Diagnostic reason, long startedNanos, int retryAfterSeconds, String sessionId)\n   443\t            throws AuditPersistenceException {\n   444\t        AuthEvent event = AuthEvent.builder()\n   445\t                .timestampMillis(time.now().toEpochMilli())\n   446\t                .type(type)\n   447\t                .outcome(outcome)\n   448\t                .account(submitted, present)\n   449\t                .source(canonicalSource)\n   450\t                .reason(reason)\n   451\t                .latencyMicros(Math.max(0L, (System.nanoTime() - startedNanos) / 1000L))\n   452\t                .retryAfterSeconds(retryAfterSeconds)\n   453\t                .sessionSuffix(sessionId)\n   454\t                .build();\n   455\t        audit.record(event);\n   456\t    }\n   457\t\n   458\t    /** Fluent construction. {@link #build()} performs every start-up check and fails closed. */\n   459\t    public static Builder builder() {\n   460\t        return new Builder();\n   461\t    }\n   462\t\n   463\t    /** Assembles the service from configuration and a time source. */\n   464\t    public static final class Builder {\n   465\t\n   466\t        private AuthConfig config;\n   467\t        private TimeSource time;\n   468\t        private SecureRandom random;\n   469\t\n   470\t        private Builder() {\n   471\t        }\n   472\t\n   473\t        public Builder config(AuthConfig value) {\n   474\t            this.config = value;\n   475\t            return this;\n   476\t        }\n   477\t\n   478\t        public Builder timeSource(TimeSource value) {\n   479\t            this.time = value;\n   480\t            return this;\n   481\t        }\n   482\t\n   483\t        /**\n   484\t         * Optional, for tests. The service is not\n   499\t         *     constructed at all: there is no partially working instance, no permissive default and no\n   500\t         *     in-memory fallback credential.\n   501\t         */\n   502\t        public AuthenticationService build() throws InvalidConfigException {\n   503\t            List<String> problems = new ArrayList<>();\n   504\t            if (config == null) {\n   505\t                problems.add(\"configuration is required\");\n   506\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   507\t            }\n   508\t            TimeSource clock = time == null ? TimeSource.systemUtc() : time;\n   509\t            config.validateFilesystem();\n   510\t\n   511\t            List<String> storeProblems = new ArrayList<>();\n   512\t            FileCredentialStore InvalidConfigException(storeProblems);\n   515\t            }\n   516\t            if (loadedStore.size() == 0) {\n   517\t                problems.add(\"credential store contains no usable records\");\n   518\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   519\t            }\n   520\t\n   521\t            AuditLogger logger = AuditLogger.open(config.eventLogFile(), config.maxEvents(),\n   522\t                    config.minFreeBytes(), clock.nowMillis());\n   523\t\n   524\t            SecureRandom generator;\n   525\t            if (random != null) {\n   526\t                generator = random;\n   527\t            } else {\n   528\t                // The platform default rather than SecureRandom.getInstanceStrong(): on Linux the\n   529\t                //             // The default instance is the JCE cryptographically strong generator (NativePRNG,\n   532\t                // seeded from /dev/urandom) and is what the platform recommends for this use.\n   533\t                generator = new SecureRandom();\n   534\t            }\n   535\t\n   536\t            char[] pepper;\n   537\t            try {\n   538\t                pepper = KeystorePepperProvider.loadPepper(config.keystoreFile(),\n   539\t                        config.keystorePassword(), config.passwordEntryAlias());\n   540\t            } catch (AuthException e) {\n   541\t                problems.add(\"the credential hash key could not be loaded from the configured keystore\");\n   542\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   543\t            }\n   544\t\n   545\t            PasswordHasher passwordHasher;\n   546\t            CredentialVerifier credentialVerifier;\n   547\t            LatencyBudget latencyBudget;\n   548\t            try problems.add(\"the credential hash key is unusable\");\n   552\t                throw new InvalidConfigException(problems);\n   553\t            } finally {\n   554\t                Arrays.fill(pepper, (char) 0);\n   555\t            }\n   556\t\n   557\t            CredentialVerifier.AttemptParameters decoy;\n   558\t            try {\n   559\t                byte[] decoySalt = AttemptThrottle();\n   577\t            return new AuthenticationService(config, loadedStore, passwordHasher, credentialVerifier,\n   578\t                    attemptThrottle, tokenStore, logger, clock, latencyBudget);\n   579\t        }\n   580\t    }\n   581\t}\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     4\timport java.security.GeneralSecurityException;\n     5\timport java.security.MessageDigest;\n     6\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n     7\timport java.util.Arrays;\n     8\timport java.util.Base64;\n     9\timport javax.crypto.Mac;\n    10\timport javax.crypto.SecretKeyFactory;\n    11\timport javax.crypto.spec.PBEKeySpec;\n    12\timport javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec;\n    13\t\n    14\t/**\n    15\t * Computes and checks the stored credential digest.\n    16\t *\n    17\t * <p>Construction: {@code digest = HMAC-SHA-256(pepper, salt || PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256(password, salt, c))}\n    18\t * with a 16-byte per-account salt from {@link SecureRandom}.\n    19\t *\n    20\t * <p>Why this shape:\n    21\t * <ul>\n    22\t *   <li>PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256 from the JDK is used rather than a hand-rolled KDF and rather than\n    23\t *       SHA-256 or a single hash pass. The JDK has no built-in Argon2id or scrypt; the trade-off is\n    24\t *       documented in the README and the iteration floor is calibrated for it.</li>\n    25\t *   <li>The pepper is an HMAC <em>key</em>, not a string appended to the password. Concatenating a\n    26\t *       pepper and a password without length prefixes lets a short password and a longer one that\n    27\t *       absorbs part of the pepper collide; a MAC key has no such boundary.</li>\n    28\t *   <li>Comparison is {@link MessageDigest#isEqual(byte[], byte[])}, which is constant time.</li>\n    29\t *   <li>The salt is normalised through the JDK Base64 codec so the bytes that were hashed and the\n    30\t *       bytes re-derived at verification time are identical - no re-encoding drift.</li>\n    31\t * </ul>\n    32\t *\n    33\t * <p>Intermediate material is wiped as soon as it is unused. Note that {@code char[]} contents\n    34\t * passed in are copied by {@link PBEKeySpec}; callers are responsible for wiping their own arrays,\n    35\t * and this class never retains a password.\n    36\t */\n    37\tfinal class PasswordHasher {\n    38\t\n    39\t    static final String KDF_ALGORITHM = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\";\n    40\t    static final String MAC_ALGORITHM = \"HmacSHA256\";\n    41\t    static final int SALT_BYTES = 16;\n    42\t    static final int DERIVED_KEY_BITS = 256;\n    43\t    static final int MIN_PEPPER_BYTES = 16;\n    44\t\n    45\t    /** Length of the stored digest, which is the HMAC-SHA-256 output. */\n    46\t    static final int MAX_DIGEST_BYTES = 32;\n    47\t\n    48\t    /** Upper bound on the work factor this class will perform in one call, independent of config. */\n    49\t    static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = 10_000_000;\n    50\t\n    51\t    private final byte[] pepper;\n    52\t    private final int iterations;\n    53\t\n    54\t    /**\n    55\t     * Hard ceiling on the work factor performed <em>per verification</em>, which is the configured\n    56\t     * count and not {@link #MAX_ITERATIONS}.\n    57\t     *\n    58\t     * <p>The iteration count is read from the credential file, so a record can order this class to do\n    59\t     * eighty times the work every other account costs - eighty times the CPU on the machine, and\n    60\t     * eighty times the delay before the latency floor releases. Two findings share that one number:\n    61\t     * the delay is an account-existence oracle that survives the uniform-work design (the equalising\n    62\t     * floor is measured against the configured cost, so a record that costs more than the floor is\n    63\t     * simply late), and it is a cheap denial of service against the four-permit work budget - a\n    64\t     * handful of such records holds every login slot. A record cannot legitimately ask for more work\n    65\t     * than the deployment's own current policy, so this class refuses it rather than performing it.\n    66\t     */\n    67\t    private final int maxSaltIterations;\n    68\t    private final SecureRandom random;\n    69\t\n    70\t    PasswordHasher(char[] pepperChars, int iterations, SecureRandom random) throws AuthException {\n    71\t        if (pepperChars == null || pepperChars.length < MIN_PEPPER_BYTES || iterations < 1) {\n    72\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    73\t        }\n    74\t        if (iterations > MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n    75\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    76\t        }\n    77\t        byte[] encoded = InputValidator.encodePassword(pepperChars);\n    78\t        if (encoded.length < MIN_PEPPER_BYTES) {\n    79\t            Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n    80\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n    81\t        }\n    82\t        this.pepper = encoded;\n    83\t        this.iterations = iterations;\n    84\t        this.maxSaltIterations = iterations;\n    85\t        this.random = random;\n    86\t    }\n    87\t\n    88\t    byte[] newSalt() {\n    89\t        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_BYTES];\n    90\t        random.nextBytes(salt);\n    91\t        return salt;\n    92\t    }\n    93\t\n    94\t    int iterations() {\n    95\t        return iterations;\n    96\t    }\n    97\t\n    98\t    /**\n    99\t     * Derives the digest to store.\n   100\t     *\n   101\t     * @param passwordUtf8 UTF-8 bytes of the password, already validated for exact encodability\n   102\t     */\n   103\t    byte[] derive(byte[] passwordUtf8, byte[] salt, int saltIterations) throws AuthException {\n   104\t        if (salt == null || salt.length != SALT_BYTES) {\n   105\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n   106\t        }\n   107\t        if (saltIterations < 1 || saltIterations > maxSaltIterations) {\n   108\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n   109\t        }\n   110\t        if (passwordUtf8 == null || passwordUtf8.length == 0) {\n   111\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n   112\t        }\n   113\t        // PBEKeySpec takes characters, so the validated UTF-8 bytes are turned back into the\n   114\t        // ISO-8859-1 view of the same bytes. This is a bijection, so it cannot merge two distinct\n   115\t        // passwords - unlike the platform default charset, which folds unrepresentable characters.\n   116\t        char[] asChars = iso88591Chars(passwordUtf8);\n   117\t        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(asChars, salt, saltIterations, DERIVED_KEY_BITS);\n   118\t        Arrays.fill(asChars, (char) 0);\n   119\t        final byte[] derived;\n   120\t        try {\n   121\t            SecretKeyFactory factory = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(KDF_ALGORITHM);\n   122\t            derived = factory.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n   123\t        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | RuntimeException e) {\n   124\t            spec.clearPassword();\n   125\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n   126\t        }\n   127\t        spec.clearPassword();\n   128\t        try {\n   129\t            Mac mac = Mac.getInstance(MAC_ALGORITHM);\n   130\t            mac.init(new SecretKeySpec(pepper, MAC_ALGORITHM));\n   131\t            mac.update(salt);\n   132\t            byte[] digest = mac.doFinal(derived);\n   133\t            Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n   134\t            return digest;\n   135\t        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | RuntimeException e) {\n   136\t            Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n   137\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n   138\t        }\n   139\t    }\n   140\t\n   141\t    /**\n   142\t     * Checks a candidate password against a stored digest in constant time with respect to the\n   143\t     * digest contents.\n   144\t     *\n   145\t     * @return {@code true} only when the candidate reproduces the stored digest\n   146\t     */\n   147\t    boolean matches(byte[] passwordUtf8, byte[] salt, int saltIterations, byte[] storedDigest) throws AuthException {\n   148\t        if (storedDigest == null || storedDigest.length != MAX_DIGEST_BYTES) {\n   149\t            // A truncated or oversized stored digest cannot be checked; treat as a store problem\n   150\t            // so the attempt fails closed instead of comparing against a padded value.\n   151\t            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n   152\t        }\n   153\t        byte[] candidate = derive(passwordUtf8, salt, saltIterations);\n   154\t        try {\n   155\t            return MessageDigest.isEqual(candidate, storedDigest);\n   156\t        } finally {\n   157\t            Arrays.fill(candidate, (byte) 0);\n   158\t        }\n   159\t    }\n   160\t\n   161\t    /**\n   162\t     * Decodes a Base64 field from the credential file, requiring the exact canonical form so that\n   163\t     * two different textual values can never denote the same salt.\n   164\t     *\n   165\t     * <p>There is deliberately no matching encoder. This component reads credential records and\n   166\t     * never writes them: rendering a record is provisioning, which is account creation, and outside\n   167\t     * the scope of an authentication component.\n   168\t     *\n   169\t     * @return the decoded bytes, or {@code null} when the text is not canonical Base64\n   170\t     */\n   171\t    static byte[] decodeBytes(String value, int expectedLength) {\n   172\t        if (value == null || value.isEmpty()) {\n   173\t            return null;\n   174\t        }\n   175\t        final byte[] decoded;\n   176\t        try {\n   177\t            decoded = Base64.getDecoder().decode(value);\n   178\t        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n   179\t            return null;\n   180\t        }\n   181\t        if (decoded.length != expectedLength) {\n   182\t            return null;\n   183\t        }\n   184\t        if (!Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(decoded).equals(value)) {\n   185\t            return null;\n   186\t        }\n   187\t        return decoded;\n   188\t    }\n   189\t\n   190\t    /**\n   191\t     * Measures how long {@code iterations} of PBKDF2 take on this machine, used by the latency\n   192\t     * budget in {@link CredentialVerifier} so the budget tracks the configured work factor instead\n   193\t     * of a hard-coded guess.\n   194\t     */\n   195\t    long measureDerivationNanos(int iterations, byte[] probePassword) {\n   196\t        byte[] salt = newSalt();\n   197\t        long best = Long.MAX_VALUE;\n   198\t        for (int i = 0; i < 2; i++) {\n   199\t            long start = System.nanoTime();\n   200\t            try {\n   201\t                derive(probePassword, salt, iterations);\n   202\t            } catch (AuthException e) {\n   203\t                return -1L;\n   204\t            }\n   205\t            long elapsed = System.nanoTime() - start;\n   206\t            if (elapsed < best) {\n   207\t                best = elapsed;\n   208\t            }\n   209\t        }\n   210\t        return best;\n   211\t    }\n   212\t\n   213\t    void erase() {\n   214\t        Arrays.fill(pepper, (byte) 0);\n   215\t    }\n   216\t\n   217\t    private static char[] iso88591Chars(byte[] bytes) {\n   218\t        char[] out = new char[bytes.length];\n   219\t        for (int i = 0; i < bytes.length; i++) {\n   220\t            out[i] = (char) (bytes[i] & 0xff);\n   221\t        }\n   222\t        return out;\n   223\t    }\n   224\t\n   225\t    static byte[] utf8(String value) {\n   226\t        return value.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n   227\t    }\n   228\t}\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.io.IOException;\n     4\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     5\timport java.nio.file.Files;\n     6\timport java.nio.file.LinkOption;\n     7\timport java.nio.file.Path;\n     8\timport java.util.ArrayList;\n     9\timport java.util.Collections;\n    10\timport java.util.LinkedHashMap;\n    11\timport java.util.List;\n    12\timport java.util.Map;\n    13\timport java.util.Optional;\n    14\t\n    15\t/**\n    16\t * Credential records loaded from the configured file.\n    17\t *\n    18\t * <p>The file is operator-supplied and therefore attacker-controlled: anyone with write access to it\n    19\t * can try to shape a security outcome, so every field is validated on load and any problem is fatal\n    20\t * at start-up rather than tolerated. No URL, ZIP or remote URI scheme is accepted anywhere; the path\n    21\t * must be an absolute local path, checked for {@code NOFOLLOW_LINKS} regular-file status before it\n    22\t * is read.\n    23\t *\n    24\t * <p>Record format, one record per line, UTF-8:\n    25\t * <pre>username : base64(16-byte salt) : iteration-count : base64(32-byte digest)</pre>\n    26\t * A valid username cannot contain {@code :}, so the split is unambiguous. Rejected outright, each\n    27\t * with a start-up problem that names only the line number and never the offending content:\n    28\t * <ul>\n    29\t *   <li>a username that fails {@link InputValidator};</li>\n    30\t *   <li>Base64 that is not canonical, or of the wrong length;</li>\n    31\t *   <li>an iteration count below the configured minimum or above the hard maximum;</li>\n    32\t *   <li>duplicate usernames, and usernames that collide with another record ignoring ASCII case -\n    33\t *       either would let one spelling name two different records, so the store refuses to load;</li>\n    34\t *   <li>more records than {@link #MAX_RECORDS}, so the file cannot be used to exhaust memory.</li>\n    35\t * </ul>\n    36\t */\n    37\tfinal class FileCredentialStore implements CredentialStore {\n    38\t\n    39\t    static final int MAX_RECORDS = 20_000;\n    40\t    private static final int MAX_FILE_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024;\n    41\t    private static final int
